Applying again Proposition
2.1
, we have

(2.86)

|1+H_{k+1}-\kappa e^{x-a_{k+1}}|\lesssim\begin{cases}e^{-2(a_{k+1}-x)}\qquad&\text{if }x\leq a_{k+1}\\
e^{x-a_{k+1}}\qquad&\text{if }x\geq a_{k+1},\end{cases}

thus, taking into account that
|\partial_{x}H_{k}(x)|+|U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k}(x))-1|\lesssim e^{-|x-a_{k}|}
,

(2.87)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\partial_{x}H_{k}(U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k})-1)(1+H_{k+1})\mathrm{\,d}x-\kappa\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\partial_{x}H_{k}(U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k})-1)e^{x-a_{k+1}}\mathrm{\,d}x\bigg|\lesssim

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{-\infty}^{a_{k+1}}e^{-2|x-a_{k}|}e^{-2(a_{k+1}-x)}\mathrm{\,d}x+\int_{a_{k+1}}^{\infty}e^{-2(x-a_{k})}e^{x-a_{k+1}}\mathrm{\,d}x.

The first integral on the right hand side is
\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}
by Lemma
2.3
,
and the second equals
e^{-2(a_{k+1}-a_{k})}
.
Hence, (
2.20
) yields

(2.88)

\bigg|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\partial_{x}H_{k}(U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k})-1)(1+H_{k+1})\mathrm{\,d}x+2\kappa^{2}e^{-(a_{k+1}-a_{k})}\bigg|\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}.

Similarly,

(2.89)

\bigg|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\partial_{x}H_{k}(U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k})-1)(1-H_{k-1})\mathrm{\,d}x+2\kappa^{2}e^{-(a_{k}-a_{k-1})}\bigg|\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}.

These two bounds, together with (
2.85
), yield (
2.76
).
∎

Remark 2.9
.

We see from Lemma
2.8
that the interaction between consecutive kink and antikink is attractive.

We turn to computing the first nontrivial term in the expansion of the energy of a multi-kink configuration.

Lemma 2.10
.

There exists
C>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for every
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
with
\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})\leq\eta_{0}

(2.90)

\displaystyle\Big|E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-Mn-\frac{1}{2}M\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}^{2}+2\kappa^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}e^{-y_{k}}\Big|\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}+y_{\min}\left\lvert{v}\right\rvert^{4}.

Proof.

First we compute the kinetic energy.

(2.91)

\begin{split}E_{\mathrm{k}}(\dot{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))&=E_{\mathrm{k}}\big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}(-v_{k}\gamma_{v_{k}}\partial_{x}H(\gamma_{v_{k}}(x-a_{k})))\big)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}(-v_{k}\gamma_{v_{k}}\partial_{x}H(\gamma_{v_{k}}(x-a_{k})))\Big)^{2}\,\mathrm{\,d}x.\end{split}

By Lemma
2.3
, the cross terms (when
j\neq k
) satisfy

(2.92)

\begin{split}\Big|v_{j}v_{k}\gamma_{v_{j}}\gamma_{v_{k}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\partial_{x}H(\gamma_{v_{k}}(x-a_{k}))\partial_{x}H(\gamma_{v_{j}}(x-a_{j}))\,\mathrm{\,d}x\Big|\lesssim|v|^{2}y_{\min}e^{-\gamma_{v_{\min}}y_{\min}},\end{split}

which is negligible since
\gamma_{v_{\min}}\geq 1
. The terms when
j=k
then satisfy

(2.93)

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}v_{k}^{2}\gamma_{v_{k}}^{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\partial_{x}H(\gamma_{v_{k}}(x-a_{k}))\Big)^{2}\,\mathrm{\,d}x=\frac{1}{2}v_{k}^{2}\gamma_{v_{k}}M\end{split}

from which we see that the kinetic energy satisfies

(2.94)

\begin{split}\Big|E_{\mathrm{k}}(\dot{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\frac{1}{2}M\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}^{2}\gamma_{v_{k}}|\lesssim|v|^{2}y_{\min}e^{-\gamma_{v_{\min}}y_{\min}}.\end{split}

From (
2.60
) and Lemma
2.3
, we have

(2.95)

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big|U(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}U(H_{k})\Big|\mathrm{\,d}x\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-\gamma_{v_{\min}}y_{\min}}.

Invoking again Lemma
2.3
, we also have

(2.96)

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big|\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{x}H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\partial_{x}H(a_{k},v_{k}))^{2}\Big|\mathrm{\,d}x\\
\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}|\partial_{x}H_{i}||\partial_{x}H_{j}|\mathrm{\,d}x\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-\gamma_{v_{\min}}y_{\min}}.

Combining these two bounds, with the identity

(2.97)

\begin{split}E_{\mathrm{p}}(H(a,v))=\gamma_{v}M-\frac{1}{2}v^{2}\gamma_{v}M,\end{split}

we obtain

(2.98)

\big|E_{\mathrm{p}}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-M\sum_{k=1}^{n}\gamma_{v_{k}}+\frac{1}{2}M\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}^{2}\gamma_{k}\big|\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-\gamma_{v_{\min}}y_{\min}}.

Combining with the kinetic energy arrive at the estimate

(2.99)

\begin{split}\Big|E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-M\sum_{k=1}^{n}\gamma_{v_{k}}\Big|\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-\gamma_{v_{\min}}y_{\min}}.\end{split}

For all
s\geq 0
, set
\vec{a}(s)\coloneqq(a_{1}+s,a_{2}+2s,\ldots,a_{n}+ns)
.
Applying the last estimate with
\vec{a}(s)
instead of
\vec{a}
, we get

(2.100)

\lim_{s\to\infty}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(s),\vec{v}))=M\sum_{k=1}^{n}\gamma_{v_{k}}.

By the Chain Rule, (
2.41
), (
2.76
), and noting the orthogonality
0=\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}(a,v),\,\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}(a,v)\rangle
we have

(2.101)

\begin{split}{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{s}}}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(s),\vec{v}))&=\langle\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(s),\vec{v})),\,\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}k\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}(a_{k},v_{k})\rangle\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}k\langle\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a}(s),\vec{v}),\,\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}(a_{k}+ks,v_{k})\rangle\\
&\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}(-1)^{j}kv_{j}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}(a_{j}+js),\,\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}(a_{k}+ks,v_{k})\rangle\\
&=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}kF_{k}(\vec{a}(s),\vec{v})+O\big(|\vec{v}|^{2}(y_{\min}+s)e^{-(y_{\min}+s)}\big)\\
&=2\kappa^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}e^{-y_{k}-s}+O\big(|\vec{v}|^{2}(y_{\min}+s)e^{-(y_{\min}+s)}\big)+O\big((y_{\min}+s)e^{-2(y_{\min}+s)}\big).\end{split}

An integration in
s
yields

(2.102)

\begin{split}\Big|E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-M\sum_{k=1}^{n}\gamma_{v_{k}}+2\kappa^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}e^{-y_{k}}\Big|\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}+y_{\min}\left\lvert{v}\right\rvert^{4}.\end{split}

Lastly, noting the small
v
estimate
\gamma_{v}=1+\frac{1}{2}v^{2}+O(v^{4})
, we obtain

(2.103)

\begin{split}\Big|E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-Mn-\frac{1}{2}M\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}^{2}+2\kappa^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}e^{-y_{k}}\Big|\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}+y_{\min}\left\lvert{v}\right\rvert^{4},\end{split}

as claimed.
∎

2.4.
Taylor expansions

We recall a few estimates based on the Taylor expansion of the nonlinearity from
[
31
]
.

Lemma 2.11
.

There exist
C,\eta>0
such that for all
-1-\eta\leq w,w^{\sharp}\leq 1+\eta
and
|g|+|g^{\sharp}|\leq\eta

the following bounds hold:

(2.104)

\displaystyle\big|U(w+g)-U(w)-U^{\prime}(w)g\big|\leq Cg^{2},

(2.105)

\displaystyle\big|U(w+g)-U(w)-U^{\prime}(w)g-U^{\prime\prime}(w)g^{2}/2\big|\leq Cg^{3},

(2.106)

\displaystyle\big|U^{\prime}(w+g)-U^{\prime}(w)-U^{\prime\prime}(w)g\big|\leq Cg^{2},

(2.107)

\displaystyle\big|U^{\prime\prime}(w+g)-U^{\prime\prime}(w)-U^{\prime\prime\prime}(w)g\big|\leq Cg^{2},

(2.108)

\displaystyle\big|\big(U^{\prime}(w^{\sharp}+g^{\sharp})-U^{\prime}(w^{\sharp})-U^{\prime\prime}(w^{\sharp})g^{\sharp}\big)-\big(U^{\prime}(w+g)-U^{\prime}(w)-U^{\prime\prime}(w)g\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|g^{\sharp}|+|g|\big)\big(\big|g^{\sharp}-g\big|+|w^{\sharp}-w|\big(|g^{\sharp}|+|g|)\big).

Proof.

Bounds (
2.104
), (
2.105
), (
2.106
) and (
2.107
) are clear,
so we are left with (
2.108
).
The Taylor formula yields

(2.109)

\displaystyle U^{\prime}(w+g)-U^{\prime}(w)-U^{\prime\prime}(w)g

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{g}sU^{\prime\prime\prime}(w+g-s)\mathrm{\,d}s,

(2.110)

\displaystyle U^{\prime}(w^{\sharp}+g^{\sharp})-U^{\prime}(w^{\sharp})-U^{\prime\prime}(w^{\sharp})g^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{g^{\sharp}}sU^{\prime\prime\prime}(w^{\sharp}+g^{\sharp}-s)\mathrm{\,d}s.

We observe that, since
U^{\prime\prime\prime}
is locally Lipschitz,

(2.111)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{g^{\sharp}}sU^{\prime\prime\prime}(w^{\sharp}+g^{\sharp}-s)\mathrm{\,d}s-\int_{0}^{g^{\sharp}}sU^{\prime\prime\prime}(w+g-s)\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg|\int_{0}^{g^{\sharp}}s|w^{\sharp}+g^{\sharp}-w-g|\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(g^{\sharp})^{2}(|w^{\sharp}-w|+|g^{\sharp}-g|).

We also have

(2.112)

\bigg|\int_{0}^{g^{\sharp}}sU^{\prime\prime\prime}(w+g-s)\mathrm{\,d}s-\int_{0}^{g}sU^{\prime\prime\prime}(w+g-s)\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|=\bigg|\int_{g}^{g^{\sharp}}sU^{\prime\prime\prime}(w+g-s)\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|\lesssim\bigg|\int_{g}^{g^{\sharp}}|s|\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|.

If
g
and
g^{\sharp}
have the same sign, then the last integral equals
\frac{1}{2}|(g^{\sharp})^{2}-g^{2}|=\frac{1}{2}(|g^{\sharp}|+|g|)|g^{\sharp}-g|
.
If
g
and
g^{\sharp}
have opposite signs, then we obtain
\frac{1}{2}((g^{\sharp})^{2}+g^{2})\leq\frac{1}{2}(|g^{\sharp}|+|g|)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(|g^{\sharp}|+|g|)|g^{\sharp}-g|
. This proves (
2.108
).
∎

2.5.
Schrödinger operator with multiple potentials

We define

(2.113)

L\coloneqq\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{p}(H)=-\partial_{x}^{2}+U^{\prime\prime}(H)=-\partial_{x}^{2}+1+(U^{\prime\prime}(H)-1).

Differentiating
\partial_{x}^{2}H(x-a)=U^{\prime}(H(x-a))
with respect to
a
we obtain

(2.114)

\big({-}\partial_{x}^{2}+U^{\prime\prime}(H(\cdot-a))\big)\partial_{x}H(\cdot-a)=0,

in particular for
a=0
we have
L(\partial_{x}H)=0
.
Since
\partial_{x}H
is a positive function,
0
is a simple eigenvalue of
L
,
which leads to the following coercivity estimate
(see
[
26
, Lemma 2.3]
for the exact same formulation, as well as
[
21
]
for a similar result).

Lemma 2.12
.

There exist
\nu,C>0
such that for all
h\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
the following inequality holds:

(2.115)

\langle h,Lh\rangle\geq\nu\|h\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-C\langle\partial_{x}H,h\rangle^{2}.

\boxslash

For any increasing
n
-tuple
\vec{a}
, and
\vec{v}\in B_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(0,1)
,
we denote
L(\vec{a},\vec{v})\coloneqq\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{p}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))
,
which is the Schrödinger operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
given by

(2.116)

(L(\vec{a},\vec{v})h)(x)\coloneqq{-}\partial_{x}^{2}h(x)+U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v};x))h(x).

Lemma 2.13
.

There exist
y_{0},\nu,C>0
such that the following holds.
Let
\vec{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfy
a_{k+1}-a_{k}\geq y_{0}
for all
k\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}

and let
h\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. Then

(2.117)

\displaystyle\langle h,L(\vec{a},\vec{0})h\rangle\geq\nu\|h\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-C\sum_{k=1}^{n}\langle\partial_{x}H_{k},h\rangle^{2}.

Proposition 2.14
.

There exist
\nu,C,\eta_{0}>0
such that for all
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
satisfying

\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})\leq\eta_{0}
and
\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}
the following inequality holds:

(2.118)

\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{0},D^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\rangle\geq\nu\|\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}-C\sum_{k=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\rangle^{2}.

We first prove Lemma
2.13
and then deduce Proposition
2.14
as a quick consequence.

Proof of Lemma
2.13
.

To simplify notation, by analogy with (
1.45
) we write
L(\vec{a})\coloneqq L(\vec{a},\vec{0})
.
We adapt
[
26
, Proof of Lemma 2.4]
.
We set

(2.119)

\displaystyle\chi_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\chi\Big(\frac{x-a_{1}}{a_{2}-a_{1}}\Big),

\displaystyle\chi_{k}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\chi\Big(\frac{x-a_{k}}{a_{k+1}-a_{k}}\Big)-\chi\Big(\frac{x-a_{k-1}}{a_{k}-a_{k-1}}\Big),\qquad\text{for }k\in\{2,\ldots,n-1\},

\displaystyle\chi_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq 1-\chi\Big(\frac{x-a_{n-1}}{a_{n}-a_{n-1}}\Big)

and we let

(2.120)

h_{k}\coloneqq\chi_{k}h,\qquad\text{for }k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}.

We have
h=\sum_{k=1}^{n}h_{k}
, hence

(2.121)

\langle h,L(\vec{a})h\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\langle h_{k},L(\vec{a})h_{k}\rangle+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\langle h_{i},L(\vec{a})h_{j}\rangle,

so it suffices to prove that

(2.122)

\displaystyle\langle h_{k},L(\vec{a})h_{k}\rangle\geq\nu\|h_{k}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-C\langle\partial_{x}H_{k},h_{k}\rangle^{2}-{o(1)\|h\|_{H^{1}}^{2}},

(2.123)

\displaystyle\langle h_{i},L(\vec{a})h_{j}\rangle{\geq{-}o(1)\|h\|_{H^{1}}^{2}}\qquad\text{whenever }i\neq j,

(2.124)

\displaystyle\big|\langle\partial_{x}H_{k},h_{k}\rangle^{2}-\langle\partial_{x}H_{k},h\rangle^{2}\big|{\leq o(1)\|h\|_{H^{1}}^{2}},

where
\nu>0
is the constant in (
2.115
) and
o(1)\to 0
as
y_{0}\to\infty
.

We first prove (
2.122
).
Without loss of generality we can assume
a_{k}=0
. We then have

(2.125)

L(\vec{a})=L+V,\qquad V\coloneqq U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H),

thus

(2.126)

\langle h_{k},L(\vec{a})h_{k}\rangle=\langle h_{k},Lh_{k}\rangle+\langle h_{k},Vh_{k}\rangle\geq\nu\|h_{k}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-C\langle\partial_{x}H,h_{k}\rangle^{2}+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\chi_{k}^{2}Vh_{k}^{2}\mathrm{\,d}x.

We only need to check that
\|\chi_{k}^{2}V\|_{L^{\infty}}\ll 1
. If
x\geq\frac{2}{3}a_{k+1}

or
x\leq\frac{2}{3}a_{k-1}
, then
\chi_{k}(x)=0
.
If
\frac{2}{3}a_{k-1}\leq x\leq\frac{2}{3}a_{k+1}
, then
|1-H_{j}(x)|\ll 1

for all
j<k
and
|1+H_{j}(x)|\ll 1
for all
j>k
, hence

(2.127)

\big|H(\vec{a};x)-(-1)^{k}H_{k}(x)\big|=\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{j}(H_{j}(x)-1)+\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}(-1)^{j}(H_{j}(x)+1)\Big|\ll 1,

which implies
|V(x)|\ll 1
.

Next, we show (
2.123
).
Observe that

(2.128)

\chi_{i}\chi_{j}U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a};x))\geq 0,\qquad\text{for all }x\in\mathbb{R}.

Indeed, if
j\neq i+1
, then
\chi_{i}(x)\chi_{j}(x)=0
for all
x
.
If
j=i+1
, then
\chi_{i}(x)\chi_{j}(x)\neq 0
only if
\frac{2}{3}a_{i}+\frac{1}{3}a_{i+1}\leq x\leq\frac{1}{3}a_{i}+\frac{2}{3}a_{i+1}
, which implies
|1-H_{\ell}(x)|\ll 1
for all
\ell\leq i

and
|1+H_{\ell}(x)|\ll 1
for all
\ell>i
, hence
|H(\vec{a};x)-(-1)^{i}|\ll 1
,
in particular
U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a};x))>0
.
Using (
2.128
) and the fact that
\|\partial_{x}\chi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\ll 1

for all
k
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle h_{i},L(\vec{a})h_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\partial_{x}(\chi_{i}h)\partial_{x}(\chi_{j}h)+U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}))\chi_{i}\chi_{j}h^{2}\Big)\mathrm{\,d}x

\displaystyle\geq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\chi_{i}\chi_{j}(\partial_{x}h)^{2}\mathrm{\,d}x-o(1)\|h\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\geq{-}o(1)\|h\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Finally, we have

(2.129)

\displaystyle\big|\langle\partial_{x}H_{k},h_{k}\rangle^{2}-\langle\partial_{x}H_{k},h\rangle^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq(\|h_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}})\big|\langle\partial_{x}H_{k},h_{k}-h\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|h\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sum_{j\neq k}\|\chi_{j}\partial_{x}H_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq o(1)\|h\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

hence (
2.124
) follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.14
.

Set

(2.130)

\begin{split}V(\vec{a},\vec{v})\coloneqq U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a})).\end{split}

In view of the definitions of
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}
(see (
2.27
)) and Lemma
2.13
it suffices to show that for
|\vec{v}|
sufficiently small we have

(2.131)

\begin{split}|\langle V(\vec{a},\vec{v})h,\,h\rangle|\leq o(1)\|h\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.\end{split}

Using the same partition of unity
\chi_{k}
from the proof of Lemma
2.13
we write
V(\vec{a},\vec{v})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}V_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
where
V_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v};x)=\chi_{k}(x)V(\vec{a},\vec{v};x)
. Similarly to the proof of Lemma
2.13
we have
\|V_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})-(U^{\prime\prime}(H(a_{k},v_{k}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H(\cdot-a_{k})))\|_{L^{\infty}}\ll 1
for each
k
. The bound (
2.131
) then follows from noting that
\|U^{\prime\prime}(H(a_{k},v_{k}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H(\cdot-a_{k})\|_{L^{\infty}}\ll 1
for each
k
, provided
|v|
is taken sufficiently small.
∎

To each pair
(\vec{a},\vec{v})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
we associate the subspace
\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})\subset\mathcal{E}
defined by

(2.132)

\begin{split}\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})\coloneqq\{\boldsymbol{h}\in\mathcal{E}\mid 0=\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle,\quad\forall k\in\{1,\dots,n\}\}.\end{split}

In other words,
\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})
is the orthogonal complement in
\mathcal{E}
of the subspace
\mathcal{N}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\coloneqq\textrm{span}\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1},\dots\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}\}
.
We note that
\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})
is a Hilbert space, and its continuous dual
\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})^{*}
can be identified with the quotient space
\mathcal{E}/\penalty\mathcal{N}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
. We denote the elements of
\mathcal{E}/\penalty\mathcal{N}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
by
[\boldsymbol{f}]
.

For each
\ell,k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
we define a
2\times 2
matrix

(2.133)

\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{\ell,k}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\langle\partial_{v_{\ell}}\boldsymbol{H}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\rangle&\langle\partial_{v_{\ell}}\boldsymbol{H}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\\
\langle\partial_{a_{\ell}}\boldsymbol{H}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\rangle&\langle\partial_{a_{\ell}}\boldsymbol{H}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\end{pmatrix},\end{split}

and the
2n\times 2n
matrix
\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
by the formulas

(2.134)

\begin{split}m_{2\ell-1,2k-1}&\coloneqq\langle\boldsymbol{\partial}_{v_{\ell}}\boldsymbol{H}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle\\
m_{2\ell-1,2k}&\coloneqq\langle\boldsymbol{\partial}_{v_{\ell}}\boldsymbol{H}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\\
m_{2\ell,2k-1}&\coloneqq\langle\boldsymbol{\partial}_{a_{\ell}}\boldsymbol{H}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle\\
m_{2\ell,2k}&\coloneqq\langle\boldsymbol{\partial}_{a_{\ell}}\boldsymbol{H}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle,\end{split}

and we note the identities

(2.135)

\begin{split}m_{2k-1,2k-1}&=\langle\boldsymbol{\partial}_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle=-\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M,\\
m_{2k,2k}&=\langle\boldsymbol{\partial}_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle=\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M,\\
m_{2k-1,2k}&=\langle\boldsymbol{\partial}_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle=0\\
m_{2k,2k-1}&=\langle\boldsymbol{\partial}_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle=0.\end{split}

and for
\ell<k
the estimates,

(2.136)

\begin{split}|m_{2\ell-1,2k-1}|\leq Cy_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}.\end{split}

Given vectors
\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we use the non-standard notation
(\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})^{\intercal}
to denote the column vector
(\lambda_{1},\mu_{1},\lambda_{2},\mu_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n},\mu_{n})^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
, and then
M(\vec{a},\vec{v})
acts on
(\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})^{\intercal}
by matrix multiplication.

Because of the formulas (
2.135
) and the estimates (
2.136
), the matrix
M(\vec{a},\vec{v})
is diagonally dominant and hence invertible provided that
\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})
is sufficiently small. An immediate consequence is that for small enough
\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})

(2.137)

\begin{split}\mathcal{N}(\vec{a},\vec{v})=\textrm{span}\{\boldsymbol{\partial}_{a_{1}}\boldsymbol{H}_{1},\dots,\partial_{a_{n}}\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\partial_{v_{1}}\boldsymbol{H}_{1},\dots,\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\},\end{split}

meaning that to each
(\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
there is a unique
(\vec{c},\vec{d})^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
such that

(2.138)

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\lambda_{k}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu_{k}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}(c_{k}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}+d_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}).\end{split}

Lemma 2.15
.

There exist
C,\eta_{0}>0
such that the following holds.
Let
(\vec{a},\vec{v})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfy
\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})\leq\eta_{0}

and let
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathcal{E}
.

(1)

There exists unique
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

(2.139)

\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}=\boldsymbol{f}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\lambda_{k}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu_{k}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}).

It satisfies the estimates

(2.140)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}},\end{split}

and for each
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}

(2.141)

\begin{split}\big|M\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}\lambda_{k}-\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{f}\rangle\big|&\leq C\Big((\sqrt{y_{\min}}e^{-y_{\min}}+|\vec{v}|)\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\Big)\\
\big|M\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}\mu_{k}+\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{f}\rangle\big|&\leq C\Big((\sqrt{y_{\min}}e^{-y_{\min}}+|\vec{v}|)\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\Big)\end{split}

(2)

For any
(\vec{\ell},\vec{m})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
there exists unique
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})\in\mathcal{E}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\langle{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle=\ell_{k}
and
\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle=m_{k}
for all
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, and

(2.142)

\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}=\boldsymbol{f}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\lambda_{k}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu_{k}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}).

It satisfies estimates

(2.143)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\ell_{k}^{2}+m_{k}^{2})\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\end{split}

and for each
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}

(2.144)

\begin{split}|\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M\lambda_{k}-\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle-\ell_{k}|&\leq C(\sqrt{y_{\min}}e^{-y_{\min}}+|\vec{v}|)\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\\
&\quad+Cy_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}\Big(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\ell_{k}^{2}+m_{k}^{2})\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\\
|\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M\mu_{k}+\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle|&\leq C(\sqrt{y_{\min}}e^{-y_{\min}}+|\vec{v}|)\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\\
&\quad+Cy_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}\Big(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\ell_{k}^{2}+m_{k}^{2})\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big).\end{split}

Remark 2.16
.

Part
1
of Lemma
2.15
is a special case of part
2
, but we state it separately for later reference.

Proof of Lemma
2.15
.

Let
\eta_{0}>0
be as in Proposition
2.14
and fix
(\vec{a},\vec{v})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
so that
\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})\leq\eta_{0}
. Let
B:\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{b})\times\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{b})\to\mathbb{R}
denote the bilinear form

(2.145)

\begin{split}B(\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{g})\coloneqq\langle\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h},\,\boldsymbol{g}\rangle.\end{split}

Integration by parts yields the bound
|B(\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{g})|\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{\mathcal{E}}
and Proposition
2.14
gives that
B
is strongly coercive on
\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})
, i.e.,
B(\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{h})\geq\nu\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}
for all
\boldsymbol{h}\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})
. Fix
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathcal{E}
and let
[\boldsymbol{f}]
denote the corresponding equivalence class of elements in
\mathcal{E}/\penalty\mathcal{N}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
. By the Lax-Milgram theorem
[
38
]
there is a unique
\boldsymbol{h}\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})
such that

(2.146)

\begin{split}B(\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{g})=\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{g}\rangle\end{split}

for all
\boldsymbol{g}\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{b})
, and moreover,
\boldsymbol{h}
satisfies the estimate,

(2.147)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq\frac{1}{\nu}\inf_{\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu}}\big\|\boldsymbol{f}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\lambda_{k}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu_{k}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k})\big\|_{\mathcal{E}}.\end{split}

Since
\langle\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}-\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{g}\rangle=0
for all
\boldsymbol{g}\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})
we can find
(\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})\in\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that,

(2.148)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}=\boldsymbol{f}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\lambda_{k}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu_{k}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}).\end{split}

To prove the triplet
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
is unique in
\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
it suffices to show that
(\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})=(\vec{0},\vec{0})
when
\boldsymbol{h}\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})=\boldsymbol{0}
and
\boldsymbol{f}=\boldsymbol{0}
. To see this, we take the inner product of the (
2.139
)
with
\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}
and
\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}
,
using (
2.30
), (
2.32
) and (
2.33
) to obtain the system,

(2.149)

\begin{split}\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle&+\langle(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k}))\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{h}\rangle=\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle\\
&\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\big),\\
\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle&+(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k}))\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{h}\rangle=\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle\\
&\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\big)\end{split}

which, using (
2.134
), and the invertibility of
\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
yields,

(2.150)

\begin{split}(\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})^{\intercal}=\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}-\langle\partial_{v_{1}}\boldsymbol{H}_{1},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle+\langle v_{1}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle+\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{1}))\partial_{v_{1}}H_{1},\,h\rangle\\
-\langle\partial_{a_{1}}\boldsymbol{H}_{1},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle+\langle v_{1}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle+\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{1}))\partial_{a_{1}}H_{1},\,h\rangle\\
\dots\\
\dots\\
-\langle\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle+\langle v_{n}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle+\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{n}))\partial_{v_{n}}H_{n},\,h\rangle\\
-\langle\partial_{a_{n}}\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle+\langle v_{n}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle+\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{n}))\partial_{a_{n}}H_{n},\,h\rangle\end{pmatrix},\end{split}

which shows that
(\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
are determined uniquely by
\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{f}
and equal to
(\vec{0},\vec{0})
when
\boldsymbol{h}=\boldsymbol{0}
and
\boldsymbol{f}=\boldsymbol{0}
. From (
2.150
) and (
2.140
) we obtain the preliminary estimates,

(2.151)

\begin{split}|\vec{\lambda}|+|\vec{\mu}|\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}.\end{split}

For the more refined estimates in (
2.141
) we record the expressions

(2.152)

\begin{split}\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M\lambda_{k}-\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle&=-\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle-\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k}))\partial_{v_{k}}H_{k},\,h\rangle\\
&\quad+\sum_{\ell\neq k}\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\big)\\
\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M\mu_{k}+\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle&=\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle+\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k}))\partial_{a_{k}}H_{k},\,h\rangle\\
&\quad-\sum_{\ell\neq k}\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\big),\end{split}

which follow directly from (
2.149
) using (
2.30
). Using (
2.151
) together with Lemma
2.3
to estimate the cross terms gives (
2.141
).

For part
2
we note that by (
2.138
) any
\boldsymbol{h}\in\mathcal{E}
admits a decomposition

(2.153)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{h}=\boldsymbol{h}^{\perp}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(c_{k}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}+d_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\end{split}

for some
\boldsymbol{h}^{\perp}\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})
and some
(\vec{c},\vec{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Fixing
(\vec{c},\vec{d})^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
by the formula

(2.154)

\begin{split}(\vec{c},\vec{d})^{\intercal}\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})^{-1}(\vec{\ell},\vec{m})^{\intercal}\end{split}

we see that part
2
is equivalent to finding unique
(\boldsymbol{h}^{\perp},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

(2.155)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}^{\perp}=\boldsymbol{\widetilde{f}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big((\lambda_{k}+c_{k})\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu_{k}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\big)\end{split}

for

(2.156)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{f}}&\coloneqq\boldsymbol{f}-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(c_{k}v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}+d_{k}v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\big)\\
&\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\begin{pmatrix}\big(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k})\big)(c_{k}\partial_{v_{k}}H_{k}+d_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}H_{k})\\
0\end{pmatrix}\end{split}

as we note the formula

(2.157)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))&\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(c_{k}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}+d_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\Big)=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})(c_{k}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}+d_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\\
&\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big((\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})(c_{k}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}+d_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\Big)\\
&=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}c_{k}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(c_{k}v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}+d_{k}v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\begin{pmatrix}\big(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k})\big)(c_{k}\partial_{v_{k}}H_{k}+d_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}H_{k})\\
0\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

The existence and uniqueness of
(\boldsymbol{h}^{\perp},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
follows by an application of part
1
and then
\boldsymbol{h}
is given by the formula (
2.153
) with
(\vec{c},\vec{d})
uniquely determined by (
2.154
).
Using (
2.118
) we obtain the following estimates for
\boldsymbol{h}
,

(2.158)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(|\lambda_{k}||\ell_{k}|+|\mu_{k}||m_{k}|)+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\ell_{k}^{2}+m_{k}^{2})\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

and arguing similarly as in part
1
we obtain the preliminary estimate

(2.159)

\begin{split}|\vec{\lambda}|+|\vec{\mu}|\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}.\end{split}

Using this in the previous line gives,

(2.160)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\ell_{k}^{2}+m_{k}^{2})\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

and thus

(2.161)

\begin{split}|\vec{\lambda}|+|\vec{\mu}|\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\ell_{k}^{2}+m_{k}^{2})\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

We refine the estimates on
\vec{\lambda}
and
\vec{\mu}
, by taking the inner product of (
2.142
) with
\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}
and
\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}
, and using (
2.32
) and (
2.33
) to obtain,

(2.162)

\begin{split}\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M\lambda_{k}-\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle-\ell_{k}&=-\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle-\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k}))\partial_{v_{k}}H_{k},\,h\rangle\\
&\quad+\sum_{\ell\neq k}\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\big)\\
\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M\mu_{k}+\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle&=\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle+\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k}))\partial_{a_{k}}H_{k},\,h\rangle\\
&\quad-\sum_{\ell\neq k}\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\big),\end{split}

from which the estimates (
2.144
) follow from the same argument used to prove (
2.141
). Note above the presence of the term
\ell_{k}
on the left which is due to the expression (
2.33
).
∎

Lemma 2.17
.

There exists a constant
C>0
with the following property. Let
\eta_{0}>0
be given by Lemma
2.15
and let
\Omega\subset\{(\vec{a},\vec{v})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\mid\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})\leq\eta_{0}\}
be an open region. Suppose that
\Omega\ni(\vec{a},\vec{v})\mapsto\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\in\mathcal{E}
is (twice) differentiable with respect to
a_{k},v_{k}
for each
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, i.e.,
\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathcal{E}
for each
k
(same for all the second partials). For each
(\vec{a},\vec{v})\in\Omega
let
(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\mu}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\in\Pi(\vec{a},\vec{v})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times R^{n}
be the unique solution to (
2.139
) given by Lemma
2.15
. Then the mapping
\Omega\ni(\vec{a},\vec{v})\mapsto(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\mu}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))
is (twice) differentiable with respect to
a_{k},v_{k}
for each
k
, and satisfies the estimates

(2.163)

\begin{split}\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}&\leq C\big(\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\big),\\
|\partial_{a_{k}}\lambda_{j}|+|\partial_{a_{k}}\mu_{j}|&\leq C\big(\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\big),\\
|\partial_{v_{k}}\lambda_{j}|+|\partial_{v_{k}}\mu_{j}|&\leq C\big(\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\big),\end{split}

and in the case of twice differentiability

(2.164)

\|\partial_{a_{j}}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{a_{j}}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{j}}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\\
\leq C\big(\|\partial_{a_{j}}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{a_{j}}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{j}}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{a_{j}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{j}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\big),

for each
j,k
.
Let
(\vec{a},\vec{v}),(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})\in\Omega
, and denote by
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
the solution to (
2.139
) associated to
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
and let
(\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp},\vec{\mu}^{\sharp})
the solution to (
2.139
) associated to
(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})
. Writing
\boldsymbol{f}=\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
and
\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}=\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})
, and
\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}=\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})
, we have

(2.165)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}-{\boldsymbol{h}}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq C\Big((\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}})(|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|+|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|)+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\Big),\end{split}

(2.166)

\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\big|(\lambda^{\sharp}_{k}-\lambda_{k})-\big((\gamma_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\rangle-(\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle\big)\big|\\
\leq C|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}})(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}})\\
+C|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}})+C\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}),

and,

(2.167)

\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}|(\mu^{\sharp}_{k}-\mu_{k})+\big((\gamma_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\rangle-(\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle\big))|\\
\leq C|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}})(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}})\\
+C|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}})+C\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}).

Finally, for each
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}

(2.168)

\begin{split}\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}&\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}})(|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|+|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|)\\
&\quad+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}},\\
\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}-\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}&\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}})(|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|+|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|)\\
&\quad+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}.\end{split}

Proof.

We first consider the difference estimates for solutions associated to different
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
. Let
(\vec{a},\vec{v}),(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})\in\Omega
, let
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
denote the solution to (
2.139
) associated to
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
, and let
(\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp},\vec{\mu}^{\sharp})
the solution to (
2.139
) associated to
(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})
. We write
\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}=\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})
. Define

(2.169)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}&\coloneqq\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{h},\quad\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}\coloneqq\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp}-\vec{\lambda},\quad\vec{\mu}^{\flat}\coloneqq\vec{\mu}^{\sharp}-\vec{\mu}.\end{split}

By Lemma
2.15
,
(\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat},\vec{\lambda}^{\flat},\vec{\mu}^{\flat})

uniquely solves

(2.170)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}=\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda^{\flat}_{k}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu^{\flat}_{k}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\end{split}

where

(2.171)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}&=\big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))\big)\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}+\big(\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{f}\big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Big(\lambda^{\sharp}_{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\big)+\mu^{\sharp}_{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\big)\Big),\end{split}

and

(2.172)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle=\ell^{\flat}_{k}\\
\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle=m^{\flat}_{k}\end{split}

and where we have used the ad hoc notation
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{j}=\boldsymbol{J}\partial_{a_{j}}\boldsymbol{H}(a^{\sharp}_{j},v^{\sharp}_{j})
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{j}=\boldsymbol{J}\partial_{v_{j}}\boldsymbol{H}(a^{\sharp}_{j},v^{\sharp}_{j})
.

The estimate (
2.143
) and (
2.161
) give

(2.173)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+|\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}|+|\vec{\mu}^{\flat}|\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\ell^{\flat}_{k})^{2}+(\mu^{\flat}_{k})^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

from which (
2.165
) follows.
Using (
2.152
) we can take the difference of the equations for
\lambda^{\sharp}_{k}
and
\lambda_{k}
to obtain

(2.174)

\begin{split}(\lambda^{\sharp}_{k}-\lambda_{k})&-\big((\gamma_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\langle\partial_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\rangle-(\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}\rangle\big)\\
&=\Big((\gamma_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}-(\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\Big)\Big(-\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle-\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k}))\partial_{v_{k}}H_{k},\,h\rangle\\
&\quad+\sum_{\ell\neq k}\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\big)\Big)\\
&\quad+(\gamma_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\Bigg[\Big(-\langle v^{\sharp}_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}\rangle-\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H^{\sharp}_{k}))\partial_{v_{k}}H^{\sharp}_{k},\,h^{\sharp}\rangle\\
&\quad+\sum_{\ell\neq k}\big(\lambda^{\sharp}_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle+\mu^{\sharp}_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle\big)\Big)\\
&\quad-\Big(-\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle-\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k}))\partial_{v_{k}}H_{k},\,h\rangle\\
&\quad+\sum_{\ell\neq k}\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\big)\Big)\Bigg].\end{split}

We call terms negligible if they are bounded by the right-hand-side of (
2.166
). Since
\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\rho(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})\leq\delta_{0}\ll 1
we have,

(2.175)

\begin{split}|(\gamma_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}-(\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}|\lesssim|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|(|v^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|),\end{split}

so the terms on the second and third line are negligible. We estimate the differences arising from the remaining terms as follows,

(2.176)

\begin{split}|\langle v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}\rangle-\langle v^{\sharp}_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k},\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}\rangle|&\lesssim\Big(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|+|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|\big((|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|)\big)\Big)\Big(\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\Big)\\
&\quad+(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|)\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\end{split}

and

(2.177)

|\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H_{k}))\partial_{v_{k}}H_{k},\,h\rangle-\langle(U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H^{\sharp}_{k}))\partial_{v_{k}}H^{\sharp}_{k},\,h^{\sharp}\rangle|\\
\lesssim\sqrt{y_{\min}}e^{-y_{\min}}\Big(\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+(\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}})(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|+|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|)\Big)

and for all
\ell\neq k
,

(2.178)

\begin{split}|\big(\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle-\lambda_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle|&\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}\Big(|\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}|+(\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}})((|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|+|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|)\Big)\\
|\big(\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle-\mu_{\ell}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\ell},\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle|&\lesssim y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}\Big(|\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}|+(\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}})((|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|+|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|)\Big).\end{split}

An application of the estimates (
2.173
) to the previous three displayed equations completes the proof of (
2.166
).
An analogous argument yields the estimates (
2.167
) for
\vec{\mu}^{\sharp}-\vec{\mu}
.

We next turn to the differentiability of
(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\mu}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))
with respect to parameters, noting that continuity with respect to parameters follows from the difference estimates proved above. Let
(\delta\boldsymbol{h},\delta\vec{\lambda},\delta\vec{\mu})
be the solution of

(2.179)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))(\delta\boldsymbol{h})&=-(U^{\prime\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\partial_{a_{k}}H(\vec{a},\vec{v})h(\vec{a},\vec{v}),0)+\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\\
&\quad+\lambda_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\delta\lambda_{j})\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}+(\delta\mu_{j})\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\end{split}

such that
\langle\delta\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\rangle=-\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\rangle
and
\langle\delta\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\rangle=-\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\rangle
for all
j
(note that
\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}=\boldsymbol{0}
for
j\neq k
). This solution exists and is unique, by Lemma
2.15

2
.
We claim that

(2.180)

(\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\partial_{a_{k}}\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\partial_{a_{k}}\vec{\mu}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))=(\delta\boldsymbol{h},\delta\vec{\lambda},\delta\vec{\mu}).

We introduce the shorthand notation
\vec{a}(\epsilon)=(a_{1},\dots,a_{k}+\epsilon,\dots a_{n})
and define

(2.181)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v};x)&\coloneqq\frac{\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v};x)-\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v};x)}{\epsilon}-\delta\boldsymbol{h}(x)\\
\vec{\lambda}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v})&\coloneqq\frac{\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})-\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a},\vec{v})}{\epsilon}-\delta\vec{\lambda}\\
\vec{\mu}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v})&\coloneqq\frac{\vec{\mu}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})-\vec{\mu}(\vec{a},\vec{v})}{\epsilon}-\delta\vec{\mu}\end{split}

It suffices now to show that

(2.182)

\begin{split}\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}(\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\lambda}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\mu}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))=(\boldsymbol{0},\vec{0},\vec{0}).\end{split}

Introducing the ad hoc notation
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,\epsilon}=\boldsymbol{J}\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}(a_{k}+\epsilon,v_{k})
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k,\epsilon}=\boldsymbol{J}\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}(a_{k}+\epsilon,v_{k})
, we see that
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\lambda}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\mu}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))
uniquely solves (
2.142
) with

(2.183)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{f}_{\epsilon}&=\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})-\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a},\vec{v})}{\epsilon}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\Big)-(U^{\prime\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\partial_{a_{k}}H(\vec{a},\vec{v}),0)\big(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v})-\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})\big)\\
&\quad+\Big(\frac{U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v}))-U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))}{\epsilon}-U^{\prime\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\partial_{a_{k}}H(\vec{a},\vec{v}),0\Big)\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})\\
&\quad+\lambda_{k}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}}{\epsilon}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\Big)+\mu_{k}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k,\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}}{\epsilon}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\Big)\\
&\quad-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\big(\lambda_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})-\lambda_{k}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})\big)-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\big(\mu_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})-\mu_{k}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v})\big)\end{split}

and

(2.184)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle&=-\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v}),\,\frac{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}}{\epsilon}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v})-\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v}),\,\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle\\
\langle\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\rangle&=0{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j\neq k\\
\langle\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle&=-\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v}),\,\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k,\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}}{\epsilon}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v})-\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}(\epsilon),\vec{v}),\,\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\\
\langle\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\rangle&=0{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j\neq k.\end{split}

Using the continuity of
(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\mu(\vec{a},\vec{v}))
with respect to the parameters, the differentiability of
\boldsymbol{f}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
, the smoothness of
U
,
\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
,
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}
, we see that
\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\|\boldsymbol{f}_{\epsilon}\|_{\mathcal{E}}=0
. Using the continuity of
\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
with respect to parameters and the smoothness of
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}
, we see that in addition

(2.185)

\begin{split}\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\langle\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\langle\boldsymbol{q}_{\epsilon}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle=0.\end{split}

Then (
2.182
) follows from (
2.143
) and (
2.161
). Having established (
2.180
) the estimates for
(\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h},\partial_{a_{k}}\vec{\lambda},\partial_{a_{k}}\vec{\mu})
now follow from (
2.179
) and Lemma
2.15

2
.

The argument for
(\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h},\partial_{v_{k}}\vec{\lambda},\partial_{v_{k}}\vec{\mu})
is identical. The estimates (
2.164
) for all the mixed second partials is similar.

Lastly, we consider the difference estimates for
\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}
and
\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}
. Let
\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}=\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}
, and
(\partial_{a_{k}}\vec{\lambda}^{\flat},\partial_{a_{k}}\vec{\mu}^{\flat})
, which we can view as solving

(2.186)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))(\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat})&=(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})))\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}\\
&\quad+(U^{\prime\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\partial_{a_{k}}H(\vec{a},\vec{v})h,0)-(U^{\prime\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))\partial_{a_{k}}H(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})h^{\sharp},0)\\
&\quad+\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{f}\\
&\quad+\lambda^{\sharp}_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}^{\sharp}-\lambda_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\mu^{\sharp}_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}^{\sharp}-\mu_{k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\\
&\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\partial_{a_{k}}\lambda^{\sharp}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}^{\sharp}-{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}})+\partial_{a_{k}}\mu^{\sharp}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j})\\
&\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\partial_{a_{k}}\lambda^{\flat}_{j})\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}+(\partial_{a_{k}}\mu^{\flat}_{j})\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\end{split}

with

(2.187)

\begin{split}\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat},\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\,\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{j}\rangle+\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{j}\rangle\\
\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\,\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{j}\rangle+\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j}\rangle\end{split}

and the estimates for
\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}
in (
2.168
) follow from Lemma
2.15
part
2
. The estimates for
\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}-\partial_{v_{k}}{\boldsymbol{h}}
follow from the same argument.
∎

3.
Preliminaries on the Cauchy problem

Since the local well-posedness theory for (
1.2
) presents no serious difficulties,
we only briefly resume it, leaving some details to the Reader.

Definition 3.1
.

If
I\subset\mathbb{R}
is an open interval,
\iota_{-},\iota_{+}\in\{-1,1\}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}=(\phi,\dot{\phi}):I\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
, then we say that
\boldsymbol{\phi}
is a solution of (
1.10
) in the energy sector
\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}}
if
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in C(I;\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}})

and (
1.10
) holds in the sense of distributions.

Choose any
(\xi,0)\in\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}}
such that
\xi\in C^{\infty}
and
\partial_{x}\xi
is of compact support,
and write
(\phi,\dot{\phi})=(\xi+\psi,\dot{\psi})
. Then
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in C(I;\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}})
is equivalent to
\boldsymbol{\psi}\in C(I;\mathcal{E})

and (
1.10
) becomes

(3.1)

{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{t}}}\begin{pmatrix}\psi\\
\dot{\psi}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\dot{\psi}\\
\partial_{x}^{2}\psi-\psi-\big(U^{\prime}(\xi+\psi)-U^{\prime}(\xi)-\psi\big)+\big(\partial_{x}^{2}\xi-U^{\prime}(\xi)\big)\end{pmatrix},

which is the linear Klein-Gordon equation for
\psi
with the forcing term
{-}\big(U^{\prime}(\xi+\psi)-U^{\prime}(\xi)-\psi\big)+\big(\partial_{x}^{2}\xi-U^{\prime}(\xi)\big)\in C(I;\mathcal{E})
. By the uniqueness of weak solutions of linear wave equations, see
[
15
]
, we have that a weak solution of (
3.1
)
is in fact a strong solution given by the Duhamel formula.

If
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{1,0},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{2,0},\ldots
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}

are states of bounded energy, then we write

\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}\to\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
if
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\to 0
in
\mathcal{E}
, and

\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
if
\dot{\phi}_{m,0}\rightharpoonup\dot{\phi}_{0}

in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\phi_{m,0}-\phi_{0}\to 0
in
L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R})
. Observe that if
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
is bounded in
\mathcal{E}
,
then
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
is equivalent to the weak convergence to
0
in the Hilbert space
\mathcal{E}
of the sequence
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
. However, our notion of weak convergence
is more general, in particular the topological class is not necessarily
preserved under weak limits as defined above.
A similar notion of weak convergence was used in Jia and Kenig
[
33
]
.

Proposition 3.2
.

(1)

For all
\iota_{-},\iota_{+}\in\{-1,1\}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}}
and
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
there exists a unique solution
\boldsymbol{\phi}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}}
of (
1.10
) such that
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
.
The energy
E(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))
does not depend on
t
.

(2)

If
\partial_{x}\phi_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and
\dot{\phi}_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, then
\partial_{x}\phi\in C(\mathbb{R};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}))\cap C^{1}(\mathbb{R};L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))
,
\dot{\phi}\in C(\mathbb{R};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}))\cap C^{1}(\mathbb{R};L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))

and (
1.6
) holds in the strong sense in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

(3)

Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{1,0},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{2,0},\ldots\in\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}\to\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}}
.
If
(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m})_{m=1}^{\infty}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}
are the solutions of (
1.10
) such that
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
,
then
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(t)\to\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the convergence being uniform on every bounded time interval.

(4)

If
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{0}|_{[x_{1},x_{2}]}={\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{0}|_{[x_{1},x_{2}]}
, then
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}(t)|_{[x_{1}+|t-t_{0}|,x_{2}-|t-t_{0}|]}={\boldsymbol{\phi}}(t)|_{[x_{1}+|t-t_{0}|,x_{2}-|t-t_{0}|]}
for all
t\in\big[t_{0}-\frac{1}{2}(x_{2}-x_{1}),t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}(x_{2}-x_{1})\big]
.

(5)

Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{1,0},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{2,0},\ldots\in\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{\iota_{-},\iota_{+}}
.
If
(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m})_{m=1}^{\infty}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}
are the solutions of (
1.10
) such that
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
,
then
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(t)\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

Statements
1
,
2
and
3
follow from energy estimates and Picard iteration, see for example
[
17
]
or
[
60
, Section X.13]

for similar results.
Since the linear Klein-Gordon equation has propagation speed equal to 1, each Picard iteration satisfies the finite propagation speed property, and
4
follows by passing to the limit.
We skip the details.

We sketch a proof of
5
.

In the first step, we argue that it can be assumed without loss of generality that
\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{E}}
is bounded and
\|\phi_{m,0}-\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\to 0
.
To this end, let
x_{m}\to-\infty
and
x_{m}^{\prime}\to\infty
be such that

(3.2)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[x_{m},x_{m}^{\prime}]}|\phi_{m,0}(x)-\phi_{0}(x)|=0.

We define a new sequence
\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}

by the formula

(3.3)

\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0}(x)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\iota_{-}&\text{for all }x\leq x_{m}-1,\\
(x_{m}-x)\iota_{-}+(1-x_{m}+x)\phi_{m,0}(x_{m})&\text{for all }x\in[x_{m}-1,x_{m}],\\
\phi_{m,0}(x)&\text{for all }x\in[x_{m},x_{m}^{\prime}],\\
(x-x_{m}^{\prime})\iota_{+}+(1-x+x_{m}^{\prime})\phi_{m,0}(x_{m}^{\prime})&\text{for all }x\in[x_{m}^{\prime},x_{m}^{\prime}+1],\\
\iota_{+}&\text{for all }x\geq x_{m}^{\prime}+1.\end{cases}

Taking into account that

(3.4)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\sup_{x\leq x_{m}}\big(|\phi_{0}(x)-\iota_{-}|+\sup_{x\geq x_{m}^{\prime}}|\phi_{0}(x)-\iota_{+}|\big)=0,

we have that
\|\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0}-\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\to 0
.
In particular, if we take
R\gg 1
, then

(3.5)

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\sup_{x\leq-R}|\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0}(x)-\iota_{-}|\ll 1,

thus

(3.6)

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{-R}|\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0}(x)-\iota_{-}|^{2}\mathrm{\,d}x\lesssim\limsup_{m\to\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{-R}U(\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0}(x))\mathrm{\,d}x<\infty,

and similarly for
x\geq R
. Hence,
\limsup_{m\to\infty}\|\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0}-\phi_{0}\|_{H^{1}}<\infty
.

Let
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}
be the solution of (
1.10
) such that

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}(t_{0})=(\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0},\dot{\phi}_{m,0})
.
By property
4
, it suffices to prove that

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}(t)\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)
for all
t
,
which finishes the first step. In the sequel, we write
\phi_{m,0}

instead of
\widetilde{\phi}_{m,0}
.

We choose
\xi
as in (
3.1
) and write
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}=(\xi,0)+\boldsymbol{\psi}_{m}
,
\boldsymbol{\phi}=(\xi,0)+\boldsymbol{\psi}
.
It suffices to prove that, for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
, any subsequence of
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{m}(t)
has a subsequence weakly converging to
\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{h}_{m}
be the solution of (
1.3
) with initial data
\boldsymbol{h}_{m}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{\psi}_{m}(t_{0})
,

\boldsymbol{h}
the solution of the same problem with initial data
\boldsymbol{h}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{\psi}(t_{0})
,
and
\boldsymbol{g}_{m}
the solution of

(3.7)

{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{t}}}\begin{pmatrix}g_{m}\\
\dot{g}_{m}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\dot{g}_{m}\\
\partial_{x}^{2}g_{m}-g_{m}-\big(U^{\prime}(\xi+\psi_{m})-U^{\prime}(\xi)-\psi_{m}\big)+\big(\partial_{x}^{2}\xi-U^{\prime}(\xi)\big)\end{pmatrix},

with the initial data
\boldsymbol{g}_{m}(t_{0})=0
.
We thus have
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{m}=\boldsymbol{h}_{m}+\boldsymbol{g}_{m}
for all
m
.

By the continuity of the free Klein-Gordon flow, we have
\boldsymbol{h}_{m}(t)\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{h}(t)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
By the energy estimates,
\boldsymbol{g}_{m}
is bounded in
C^{1}(I;\mathcal{E})
for any bounded open interval
I
.
Since the weak topology on bounded balls of
\mathcal{E}
is metrizable, the Arzelà-Ascoli theorem yields
\boldsymbol{g}\in C(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{E})

and a subsequence of
(\boldsymbol{g}_{m})_{m}
, which we still denote
(\boldsymbol{g}_{m})_{m}
, such that
\boldsymbol{g}_{m}(t)\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{g}(t)
for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{g}
, so that
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{m}(t)\rightharpoonup\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(t)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
In particular,
\psi_{m}(t,x)\to\widetilde{\psi}(t,x)
for all
(t,x)
, hence by the dominated convergence theorem

(3.8)

U^{\prime}(\xi+\psi_{m})-U^{\prime}(\xi)-\psi_{m}\to U^{\prime}(\xi+\widetilde{\psi})-U^{\prime}(\xi)-\widetilde{\psi}

in the sense of distributions. We can thus pass to the distributional limit in (
3.1
) and conclude that
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}

is a solution of (
1.10
) with initial data
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(t_{0})=\boldsymbol{\psi}(t_{0})
.
By the uniqueness of weak solutions,
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\psi}}=\boldsymbol{\psi}
.
∎

Finally, we have local stability of vacuum solutions.

Lemma 3.3
.

There exist
\eta_{0},C_{0}>0
having the following property.
If
\boldsymbol{\iota}\in\{\boldsymbol{1},-\boldsymbol{1}\}
,
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
-\infty\leq x_{1}+1<x_{2}-1\leq\infty
,
E(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0})<0
,
\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\iota}\|_{\mathcal{E}(x_{1},x_{2})}\leq\eta_{0}
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}
is a solution of (
1.10
) such that
\boldsymbol{\phi}(0)=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}
, then for all
t\in\big[t_{0}-\frac{1}{2}(x_{2}-x_{1})+1,t_{0}+\frac{1}{2}(x_{2}-x_{1})-1\big]

(3.9)

\|\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)-\boldsymbol{\iota}\|_{\mathcal{E}(x_{1}+|t-t_{0}|,x_{2}-|t-t_{0}|)}\leq C_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\iota}\|_{\mathcal{E}(x_{1},x_{2})}.

Sketch of a proof.

To fix ideas, assume
\iota=1
and
t>t_{0}=0
.
The positivity of
U
and Green’s formula in space-time imply that the function

(3.10)

\big[0,(x_{2}-x_{1})/2-1\big]\owns t\mapsto\int_{x_{1}+t}^{x_{2}-t}\Big(\frac{1}{2}(\dot{\phi}(t))^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{x}\phi(t))^{2}+U(\phi(t))\Big)\mathrm{\,d}x

is decreasing.
Set
\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)-\boldsymbol{1}
.
For
|\psi|
small, we have
U(1+\psi)\simeq\psi^{2}
.
By a continuity argument and the fact that
\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(I)}\lesssim\|\psi\|_{H^{1}(I)}

on any interval
I
of length
\geq 2
, we obtain

\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{1}+t,x_{2}-t)}\lesssim\eta_{0}
and (
3.9
).
∎

4.
Estimates on the modulation parameters

Our present goal is to reduce the motion of a kink cluster to a system of ordinary
differential equations with sufficiently small error terms.

4.1.
Basic modulation

We consider a solution of (
1.10
) which is close, on some open time interval
I
, to multikink configurations.
It is natural to express the solution as the sum of a multikink and a small error,
which can be done in multiple ways. A unique choice of such a decomposition is obtained
by imposing specific
orthogonality conditions
. In this section, we prove Lemma
1.5
, which we state again, while including an additional estimate.

If
(\vec{a},\vec{v})\in C^{1}(I;\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
and, using the notation (
1.20
), we decompose

(4.1)

\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)=\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\boldsymbol{g}(t),

then the Chain Rule implies that
\boldsymbol{\phi}
solves (
1.10
) if and only if
\boldsymbol{g}
solves

(4.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{g}(t)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}E\big(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\boldsymbol{g}(t)\big)-{\vec{a}\,}^{\prime}(t)\cdot\partial_{\vec{a}}\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))-{\vec{v}\,}^{\prime}(t)\cdot\partial_{\vec{v}}\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))

We impose the orthogonality conditions, which we rewrite as

(4.3)

\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{g}(t)\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{g}(t)\rangle=0,\qquad\text{for all }k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\text{ and }t\in I.

Indeed, we prove the following “static” modulation lemma.

Lemma 4.1

(Modulation parameters)
.

There exist
\eta_{0},\eta_{1},C_{0}>0
with the following properties. For all
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{1,(-1)^{n}}
such that

(4.4)

\begin{split}{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0})<\eta_{0},\end{split}

there exist unique
(\vec{a},\vec{v})=(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}))\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
a_{1}\leq a_{2}\leq\dots\leq a_{n}
such that

(4.5)

\begin{split}\big\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\big\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}+\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})<\eta_{1},\end{split}

and

(4.6)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}(a_{k},v_{k})\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}),\,\boldsymbol{\beta}(a_{k},v_{k})\rangle=0,\end{split}

for all
k=1,\dots,n
. Moreover, the pair
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
satisfy the estimates

(4.7)

\begin{split}\big\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\big\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}&+\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})<C_{0}{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}).\end{split}

and there exists
\nu>0,C>0
such that

(4.8)

\frac{1}{2}\|\dot{\phi}_{0}-\dot{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\nu}{4}\|\phi_{0}-H(\vec{a},\vec{v})\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{M}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}^{2}\\
\leq 2\kappa^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}e^{-y_{k}}+Cy_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}+Cy_{\min}\left\lvert{v}\right\rvert^{4}+E(\boldsymbol{\phi})-nM.

Finally, the map
\mathcal{E}_{1,(-1)^{n}}\owns\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\mapsto(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}))\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
is
C^{1}
.

Proof.

Let
\eta\coloneqq{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
. We can choose a pair
(\vec{b},\vec{w})
so that

(4.9)

\begin{split}\eta\leq\big\|\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})\big\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}e^{-(b_{k+1}-b_{k})}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}|w_{k}|^{2}\leq 4\eta.\end{split}

for each
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
we define the functions

(4.10)

\begin{split}A_{2k-1}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h})&=\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}(a_{k},v_{k}),\,\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\rangle\\
B_{2k}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h})&=\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(a_{k},v_{k}),\,\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\rangle.\end{split}

and for
k=1,\dots,n
define
\vec{\Gamma}=(\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{2n}):C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathcal{E};\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
by

(4.11)

\begin{split}\Gamma_{\ell}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h})\coloneqq\begin{cases}A_{2k-1}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h}){\ \ \text{if}\ \ }\ell=2k-1\\
B_{2k}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h}){\ \ \text{if}\ \ }\ell=2k\end{cases}\end{split}

Using the usual shorthand
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\boldsymbol{H}_{k}
we compute

(4.12)

\begin{split}\partial_{a_{j}}A_{2k-1}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h})&=\begin{cases}\langle\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\rangle{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j=k\\
-(-1)^{j}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\,\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{j}\rangle{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j\neq k\end{cases}\\
\partial_{v_{j}}A_{2k-1}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h})&=\begin{cases}\langle\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\rangle-(-1)^{k}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\,\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j=k\\
-(-1)^{j}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\,\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{j}\rangle{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j\neq k\end{cases}\end{split}

and

(4.13)

\begin{split}\partial_{a_{j}}B_{2k}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h})&=\begin{cases}\langle\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\rangle-(-1)^{k}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\,\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j=k\\
-(-1)^{j}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\,\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{j}\rangle{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j\neq k\end{cases}\\
\partial_{v_{j}}B_{2k}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h})&=\begin{cases}\langle\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\rangle{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j=k\\
-(-1)^{j}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\,\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{j}\rangle{\ \ \text{if}\ \ }j\neq k\end{cases}\end{split}

We see that
\partial_{(\vec{a},\vec{v})}\vec{\Gamma}(\vec{b},\vec{w};\boldsymbol{h})
is a
2n\times 2n
invertible matrix with uniformly bounded inverse as long as
\boldsymbol{h}
,
|\vec{a}-\vec{b}|
and
|\vec{v}-\vec{w}|
are small enough.

Let
\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})
. Let
C_{1}>0
be a constant to be fixed below and consider, the mapping
\vec{\Phi}:B_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\vec{b},C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}})\times B_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\vec{w},C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}})\to\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
defined by

(4.14)

\begin{split}\vec{\Phi}(\vec{a},\vec{v}):=(\vec{a},\vec{v})-\big[\partial_{(\vec{a},\vec{v})}\vec{\Gamma}(\vec{b},\vec{w};\boldsymbol{h}_{0})\big]^{-1}\vec{\Gamma}(\vec{a},\vec{v};\boldsymbol{h}_{0}).\end{split}

We set up a contraction mapping to find a fixed point of
\vec{\Phi}
.
Observe that

(4.15)

\begin{split}|\vec{\Phi}(\vec{b},\vec{w};\boldsymbol{h})-(\vec{b},\vec{w})|\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{E}}=\|\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b},\vec{w})\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq 2\sqrt{\eta}\end{split}

and hence
C_{1}
can be chosen so that

(4.16)

\begin{split}|\vec{\Phi}(\vec{b},\vec{w};\boldsymbol{h})-(\vec{b},\vec{w})|\leq\frac{1}{3}C_{1}\sqrt{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{3}C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}}.\end{split}

By the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus,

(4.17)

\begin{split}&\vec{\Phi}(\vec{a^{\sharp}},\vec{v^{\sharp}})-\vec{\Phi}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\\
&=\Big[\operatorname{Id}-\big[\partial_{(\vec{a},\vec{v})}\vec{\Gamma}((\vec{b},\vec{w});\boldsymbol{h}_{0})\big]^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{(\vec{a},\vec{v})}\vec{\Gamma}(s(\vec{a^{\sharp}},\vec{v^{\sharp}})+(1-s)(\vec{a},\vec{v});\boldsymbol{h}_{0})\,\mathrm{\,d}s\Big]\Big((\vec{a^{\sharp}},\vec{v^{\sharp}})-(\vec{a},\vec{v})\Big)\end{split}

From (
4.12
), (
4.13
) we see that
\partial_{(\vec{a},\vec{v})}\vec{\Gamma}((\vec{a},\vec{v});\boldsymbol{h}_{0})
is Lipshitz with respect to
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
and hence,

(4.18)

\begin{split}|\partial_{(\vec{a},\vec{v})}\vec{\Gamma}(s(\vec{a^{\sharp}},\vec{v^{\sharp}})+(1-s)(\vec{a},\vec{v});\boldsymbol{h}_{0})-\partial_{(\vec{a},\vec{v})}\vec{\Gamma}((\vec{b},\vec{w});\boldsymbol{h}_{0})|\lesssim C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}}\end{split}

and thus,

(4.19)

\begin{split}|\vec{\Phi}(\vec{a^{\sharp}},\vec{v^{\sharp}})-\vec{\Phi}(\vec{a},\vec{v})|\lesssim C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}}|(\vec{a^{\sharp}},\vec{v^{\sharp}})-(\vec{a},\vec{v})|\leq\frac{1}{3}C_{1}|(\vec{a^{\sharp}},\vec{v^{\sharp}})-(\vec{a},\vec{v})|\end{split}

provided
\eta_{0}
is chosen small enough. Hence
\vec{\Phi}
is a strict contraction on the ball
B_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\vec{b},C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}})\times B_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\vec{w},C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}})
.

Let
(\vec{a},\vec{v})=(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi})
be the unique fixed point of
\vec{\Phi}
inside the ball
B_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\vec{b},C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}})\times B_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\vec{w},C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}})
. The orthogonality conditions (
4.6
) hold for this choice of
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
because of the definitions of
\vec{\Gamma}
and
\vec{\Phi}
. Next, using that
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
is a fixed point of
\vec{\Phi}
, (
4.15
), and (
4.18
) we see that

(4.20)

\begin{split}|(\vec{a},\vec{v})-(\vec{b},\vec{w})|=|\vec{\Phi}(\vec{a},\vec{v})-\vec{\Phi}(b,w)+\vec{\Phi}(b,w)-(\vec{b},\vec{w})|\leq\frac{1}{3}|(\vec{a},\vec{v})-(\vec{b},\vec{w})|+\frac{1}{3}C_{1}\sqrt{\eta}\end{split}

and hence
|(\vec{a},\vec{v})-(\vec{b},\vec{w})|\lesssim\sqrt{\eta}=\sqrt{{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi})}
. The estimates (
4.7
) readily follow.

We now prove (
4.8
). Set
\boldsymbol{g}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\phi}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
.
We have the Taylor expansion

(4.21)

E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})+\boldsymbol{g})=E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))+\langle\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})),\boldsymbol{g}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{g},\boldsymbol{g}\rangle\\
+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(U(H(\vec{a},\vec{v})+g)-U(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))g-\frac{1}{2}U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))g^{2}\Big)\mathrm{\,d}x.

Lemma
2.13
and (
4.6
) yield

(4.22)

\langle\boldsymbol{g},\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{g}\rangle=\|\dot{g}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle g,\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{p}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))g\rangle\geq\|\dot{g}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\|g\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

The Sobolev embedding implies that the second line has absolute value
\lesssim\|g\|_{H^{1}}^{3}
, thus
\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|g\|_{H^{1}}^{2}
if
\eta_{0}
is small enough.
By (
2.67
) and (
4.6
), we also have

(4.23)

|\langle\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})),\boldsymbol{g}\rangle|\lesssim\sqrt{y_{\min}}e^{-y_{\min}}\|g\|_{L^{2}},

thus
|\langle\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})),\boldsymbol{g}\rangle|\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|g\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+Cy_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}
.
We arrive at the estimate

(4.24)

\frac{1}{2}\|\dot{g}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\nu}{4}\|g\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\leq E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})+\boldsymbol{g})-E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))+Cy_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}.

Lemma
2.10
then implies

(4.25)

\frac{1}{2}\|\dot{g}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\nu}{4}\|g\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{M}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}^{2}\\
\leq 2\kappa^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}e^{-y_{k}}+Cy_{\min}e^{-2y_{\min}}+y_{\min}\left\lvert{v}\right\rvert^{4}+E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a})+\boldsymbol{g})-nM,

in particular (
4.8
).

Continuous differentiability of
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\mapsto(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}))
follows from
\vec{\Phi}\in C^{1}
, see
[
6
, Chapter 2, Theorem 2.2]
.

It remains to prove that, if
\eta_{1}
is small enough,
then there is no
(\vec{a},\vec{v})\notin B_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}((\vec{b},\vec{w}),C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}})
satisfying (
4.5
). Suppose the contrary. Then there exist
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0,m}
,
(\vec{b}_{m},\vec{w}_{m})
and

(\vec{a}_{m},v_{m})
such that

(4.26)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0,m}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b}_{m},\vec{w}_{m})\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}+\rho(\vec{b}_{m},\vec{w}_{m})\leq 2{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0,m})\qquad\text{for all }m,

(4.27)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\big(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0,m}-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}_{m},\vec{v}_{m})\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}+\rho(\vec{a}_{m},\vec{v}_{m})\big)=0,

(4.28)

\displaystyle|\vec{a}_{m}-\vec{b}_{m}|+|\vec{v}_{m}-\vec{w}_{m}|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{\eta_{0}}\qquad\text{for all }m.

In particular, the first two conditions above yield

(4.29)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\big(\|\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}_{m},\vec{v}_{m})-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{b}_{m},\vec{w}_{m})\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}+\rho(\vec{a}_{m},\vec{v}_{m})+\rho(\vec{b}_{m},\vec{w}_{m})\big)=0,

which contradicts the third condition. For the details of this last point, see for example
[
28
, proof of Lemma 2.27]
for a similar argument. ∎

Lemma 4.2
.

There exist
\eta_{0},C_{0},C_{1}>0
such that the following is true.
Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be an open interval and let
\boldsymbol{\phi}:I\to\mathcal{E}_{1,(-1)^{n}}
be a solution of (
1.10
) such that

(4.30)

{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))\leq\eta_{0},\qquad\text{for all }t\in I.

Then there exist
\vec{a}\in C^{1}(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\boldsymbol{g}\in C(I;\mathcal{E})

satisfying (
4.1
) and (
4.3
).
In addition, for all
t\in I
such that

(4.31)

{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))\geq C_{1}(E(\boldsymbol{\phi})-nM)

the following bounds hold:

(4.32)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{g}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}\leq C_{0}\rho(t)\leq C_{0}^{2}{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)),

(4.33)

\displaystyle\big|a_{k}^{\prime}(t)-v_{k}(t)\big|+\big|v_{k}^{\prime}(t)\big|\leq C_{0}\rho(t).

Finally, if
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}
is a sequence of solutions of (
1.10
) such that

(4.34)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}-\boldsymbol{\phi}\|_{C(I;\mathcal{E})}=0,

then the corresponding
\vec{a}_{m}
,
\vec{v}_{m}
and
\boldsymbol{g}_{m}
satisfy

(4.35)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\big(\|\vec{a}_{m}-\vec{a}\|_{C^{1}(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\vec{v}_{m}-\vec{v}\|_{C^{1}(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{m}-\boldsymbol{g}\|_{C(I;\mathcal{E})}\big)=0.

Remark 4.3
.

In Sections
6
and
7
,
we will always have
E(\boldsymbol{\phi})=nM
,
hence (
4.31
) will be automatically satisfied.
In Section
8
, an additional argument will be necessary in order to ensure (
4.31
) on time intervals of interest, see the beginning of the proof of Theorem
4
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Step 1.
(Modulation equations.)
For every
t\in I
, let
\vec{a}(t)\coloneqq\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))
and
\vec{v}(t)\coloneqq\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))
be given by Lemma
4.1
, and let
\boldsymbol{g}(t)
be given by (
4.1
).
The maps
t\mapsto\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)\in\mathcal{E}_{1,(-1)^{n}}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1,(-1)^{n}}\owns\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\mapsto\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0})
and
\mathcal{E}_{1,(-1)^{n}}\owns\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\mapsto\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0})
are continuous, hence
\vec{a}\in C(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\vec{v}\in C(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
Moreover, (
4.34
) implies

(4.36)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\big(\|\vec{a}_{m}-\vec{a}\|_{C(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\vec{v}_{m}-\vec{v}\|_{C(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{m}-\boldsymbol{g}\|_{C(I;\mathcal{E})}\big)=0.

We claim that
\vec{a}\in C^{1}(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
\vec{v}\in C^{1}(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})
and that for any
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
we have

(4.37)
