t\in[0,\widetilde{T}_{m}]
, but still
\lim_{m\to\infty}{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(\widetilde{T}_{m}))=0
.
This condition allows to apply Lemma
4.2
even though we do not assume
E(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m})=nM
.
Below, we write
T_{m}
instead of
\widetilde{T}_{m}
.

Let
y_{m,k}\coloneqq a_{m,k+1}-a_{m,k}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(t)\coloneqq\rho(\vec{a}_{m}(t))
,
as in Sections
4
and
6
.
Let
\beta_{m}:[0,T_{m}]\to\mathbb{R}
be the function defined as in the proof of Lemma
6.4
, corresponding to the solution
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}
. By the same argument,
\beta(t)

is decreasing for
t\in[0,T_{m}]
, hence

(8.3)

\beta(t)\geq-\epsilon_{m},\qquad\text{for all }t\in[0,T_{m}],

where
\epsilon_{m}\coloneqq\beta(T_{m})\to 0
as
m\to\infty
.

Fix
t>0
. For
m
large enough and all
\tau\in[0,t]
,
we have
|{\widetilde{\rho}_{m}}\,\!\!\!\!^{\prime}\,(\tau)|\lesssim\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}}
, hence
\big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{t}}}(\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big|\lesssim 1
. Since
\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(0)\gtrsim\eta
, we obtain
\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\eta^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\tau
, hence

(8.4)

\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(\tau)\gtrsim\frac{1}{\eta^{-1}+t^{2}}\qquad\text{for all }m\text{ large enough and }\tau\in[0,t].

The proof of (
6.32
) yields

(8.5)

{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{t}}}\big((\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(\tau))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\gtrsim{-}\epsilon_{m}\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\gtrsim{-}\epsilon_{m}(\eta^{-\frac{3}{2}}+t^{3}).

Integrating in time, we deduce that there is a constant
C_{2}
such that

(8.6)

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\widetilde{\rho}_{m}(t)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{\eta^{-1}+t^{2}}.

Upon extracting a subsequence, we can assume that
\lim_{m\to\infty}(a_{m,k+1}(0)-a_{m,k}(0))\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}
exists for all
k
.
Observe that at least one of these limits has to be finite, due to the bound (
4.32
) and the fact that
{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(0))=\eta
for all
m
.
We set
n^{(0)}\coloneqq 0
and define inductively

(8.7)

n^{(j)}\coloneqq\max\big\{k:\lim_{m\to\infty}a_{m,k}(0)-a_{m,n^{(j-1)}+1}(0)<\infty\big\},

until we reach
n^{(\ell)}=n
for some
\ell
. We set
X_{m}^{(j)}\coloneqq a_{m,n^{(j)}}(0)
,
so that
\lim_{m\to\infty}\big(a_{m,n^{(j-1)}+k}(0)-X_{m}^{(j)}\big)\in\mathbb{R}

for all
k\in\big\{1,\ldots,n^{(j)}-n^{(j-1)}\big\}
.
By the definition of
n^{(j)}
, conclusion (ii) of the theorem holds.

Again extracting a subsequence and applying the Arzelà-Ascoli theorem,
we can assume that
a_{m,n^{(j-1)}+k}(t)-X_{m}^{(j)}

and
v_{m,n^{(j-1)}+k}(t)
converge uniformly
on every bounded time interval. Hence, we can define

(8.8)

\displaystyle\vec{a}\,^{(j)}:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}^{n^{(j)}-n^{(j-1)}},\qquad a\,^{(j)}_{k}(t)\coloneqq\lim_{m\to\infty}\big(a_{m,n^{(j-1)}+k}(t)-X_{m}^{(j)}\big).

and

(8.9)

\displaystyle\vec{v}\,^{(j)}:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}^{n^{(j)}-n^{(j-1)}},\qquad v\,^{(j)}_{k}(t)\coloneqq\lim_{m\to\infty}v_{m,n^{(j-1)}+k}(t).

Note that
a\,^{(j)}_{n^{(j)}-n^{(j-1)}}(0)=0
for all
j
.
From the bound (
8.6
) we get
\rho\big(\vec{a}\,^{(j)}\big)\lesssim(\eta^{-1}+t^{2})^{-1}
.

For
0\leq j\leq\ell
, let
\iota^{(j)}\coloneqq(-1)^{n^{(j)}}
,
\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j)}\coloneqq(\iota^{(j)},0)
and for
1\leq j<\ell
, let
I_{m}^{(j)}\coloneqq\big[\frac{8}{9}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{1}{9}X_{m}^{(j+1)},\frac{1}{9}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{8}{9}X_{m}^{(j+1)}\big]
.
For fixed
t>0
, we have
\lim_{m\to\infty}\|H(\vec{a}_{m}(t),\vec{v}_{m}(t))-\iota^{(j)}\|_{H^{1}(I_{m}^{(j)})}=0
,
thus (
8.6
) yields

(8.10)

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(t)-\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j)}\|_{\mathcal{E}(I_{m}^{(j)})}^{2}\lesssim(\eta^{-1}+t^{2})^{-1}.

Let
J_{m}^{(j)}\coloneqq\big[\frac{5}{6}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{1}{6}X_{m}^{(j+1)},\frac{1}{6}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{5}{6}X_{m}^{(j+1)}\big]
.
By (
8.10
) and Lemma
3.3
, we obtain

(8.11)

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(0)-\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j)}\|_{\mathcal{E}(J_{m}^{(j)})}^{2}\lesssim(\eta^{-1}+t^{2})^{-1}.

Letting
t\to\infty
, we get

(8.12)

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(0)-\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j)}\|_{\mathcal{E}(J_{m}^{(j)})}^{2}=0.

Next, we divide the initial data
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(0)
into
\ell
regions in the following way. We set

(8.13)

\displaystyle\chi^{(1)}_{m}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\chi\Big(\frac{x-X^{(1)}_{m}}{X^{(2)}_{m}-X^{(1)}_{m}}\Big),

\displaystyle\chi^{(j)}_{m}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\chi\Big(\frac{x-X^{(j)}_{m}}{X^{(j+1)}_{m}-X^{(j)}_{m}}\Big)-\chi\Big(\frac{x-X^{(j-1)}_{m}}{X^{(j)}_{m}-X^{(j-1)}_{m}}\Big),\qquad\text{for }j\in\{2,\ldots,\ell-1\},

\displaystyle\chi^{(\ell)}_{m}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq 1-\chi\Big(\frac{x-X^{(\ell)}_{m}}{X^{(\ell)}-X^{(\ell-1)}_{m}}\Big)

(in the case
\ell=1
, we set
\chi^{(1)}_{m}(x)\coloneqq 1
).
We now define

(8.14)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(j)}_{m,0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\chi^{(i)}_{m}+\chi^{(j)}_{m}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(0)+\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j)}\sum_{i=j+1}^{\ell}\chi^{(i)}_{m},

\displaystyle\text{for }j\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}

(note that the two sums above are telescopic sums).
From (
8.12
), we deduce

(8.15)

\begin{gathered}\lim_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j-1)}\|_{\mathcal{E}(-\infty,\frac{5}{6}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{1}{6}X_{m}^{(j-1)})}=0,\\
\lim_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j)}\|_{\mathcal{E}(\frac{5}{6}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{1}{6}X_{m}^{(j+1)},\infty)}=0.\end{gathered}

Let
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}^{(j)}
be the solution of (
1.10
) for the initial data

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}^{(j)}(0)=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}^{(j)}
.
Let

(8.16)

\displaystyle K_{m}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big({-}\infty,\frac{7}{9}X_{m}^{(1)}+\frac{2}{9}X_{m}^{(2)}\big],

\displaystyle K_{m}^{(j)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big[\frac{7}{9}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{2}{9}X_{m}^{(j-1)},\frac{7}{9}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{2}{9}X_{m}^{(j+1)}\big]\qquad\text{for }j\in\{2,\ldots,\ell-1\},

\displaystyle K_{m}^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big[\frac{7}{9}X_{m}^{(\ell)}+\frac{2}{9}X_{m}^{(\ell-1)},\infty\big).

By the finite speed of propagation, see Proposition
3.2

4
,
and the definition of
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}^{(j)}
, we have

(8.17)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}^{(j)}(t)|_{K_{m}^{(j)}}=\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(t)|_{K_{m}^{(j)}}\qquad\text{for any given }t\geq 0\text{ and }m\text{ large enough},

hence (
8.6
) and (
4.8
) imply

(8.18)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}^{(j)}(t)-\iota^{(j-1)}\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}\,^{(j)}(t),\vec{v}\,^{(j)}(t);\cdot-X_{m}^{(j)})\|_{\mathcal{E}(K_{m}^{(j)})}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta^{-1}+t^{2}}.

For any given
t\geq 0
, Lemma
3.3
and (
8.15
) yield

(8.19)

\begin{gathered}\lim_{m\to\infty}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}^{(j)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j-1)}\|_{\mathcal{E}(-\infty,\frac{7}{9}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{2}{9}X_{m}^{(j-1)})}=0,\\
\lim_{m\to\infty}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}^{(j)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j)}\|_{\mathcal{E}(\frac{7}{9}X_{m}^{(j)}+\frac{2}{9}X_{m}^{(j+1)},\infty)}=0.\end{gathered}

Invoking (
8.18
), we obtain that for all
t\geq 0

(8.20)

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\big\|\iota^{(j-1)}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\phi}}_{m}^{(j)}\big(t,\cdot+X_{m}^{(j)}\big)-\boldsymbol{H}\big(\vec{a}\,^{(j)}(t),\vec{v}\,^{(j)}(t)\big)\big\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta^{-1}+t^{2}}.

After extraction of a subsequence, we can assume that, for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
,

(8.21)

\iota^{(j-1)}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}^{(j)}\big(\cdot+X_{m}^{(j)}\big)\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{P}_{0}^{(j)}\in\mathcal{E}_{1,\iota^{(j-1)}\iota^{(j)}}.

Let
\boldsymbol{P}^{(j)}
be the solution of (
1.10
) such that
\boldsymbol{P}^{(j)}(0)=\boldsymbol{P}^{(j)}_{0}
.
By Proposition
3.2

5
and (
8.20
),
\boldsymbol{P}^{(j)}
is a kink cluster,
so conclusion (i) of the theorem holds.
In particular,
E(\boldsymbol{P}^{(j)})=(n^{(j)}-n^{(j-1)})M
.
From (
8.11
) and (
8.14
), we have

(8.22)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}E(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}^{(j)})=\lim_{m\to\infty}E(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m})=nM=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}E(\boldsymbol{P}^{(j)}).

By the last part of Lemma
8.1
, we thus have strong convergence in (
8.21
)
for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
.
Elementary algebra yields

(8.23)

\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m}(0)=\boldsymbol{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\big(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{m,0}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\iota}^{(j-1)}\big),

implying conclusion (iii) of the theorem.
∎

9.
Well-posedness of the linearized problem

If
I\subset\mathbb{R}
is a time interval and
X
a normed space, we denote by

C(I;X)
the space of continuous functions
I\to X
,
and
C_{0}(I;X)
its (non-closed) subspace of functions with compact support
J\subset I
.
If
I
is open and
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,\infty\}
, we denote by

C^{k}(I;X)
the space of
k
times continuously differentiable functions
I\to X
,
and
C_{0}^{k}(I;X)
its subspace of functions with compact support
J\subset I
.
If
X
is a Banach space,
we denote by
L^{p}(I;X)
the usual Lebesgue space of
X
-valued functions on
I
,
see
[
13
, III.3]
.
If
1\leq p<\infty
, then
C_{0}(I;X)
is dense in
L^{p}(I;X)
, see
[
13
, IV.8.19]
.
It follows that if
Y\subset X
is a dense subspace, then
C_{0}(I;Y)
is dense in
L^{p}(I;X)
,
since any element of
C_{0}(I;X)
can be approximated by
Y
-valued piecewise affine functions.

We denote the free energy

(9.1)

E_{0}(\boldsymbol{h}_{0})\coloneqq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\dot{h}_{0}(x)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{x}h_{0}(x))^{2}+\frac{1}{2}h_{0}(x)^{2}\Big)\mathrm{\,d}x.

The Klein-Gordon equation
\partial_{t}^{2}h(t,x)=\partial_{x}^{2}h(t,x)-h(t,x)

can be rewritten in the Hamiltonian form as

(9.2)

\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t)=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}E_{0}(\boldsymbol{h}(t))=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{0}(0)\boldsymbol{h}(t).

We denote the free propagator by
\boldsymbol{S}_{0}(t)
, which is given by

(9.3)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{S}_{0}(t)=e^{tJ\mathrm{D}E_{0}}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos(t\omega)&\omega^{-1}\sin(t\omega)\\
-\omega\sin(t\omega)&\cos(t\omega)\end{pmatrix}\end{split}

where we have used the short-hand notation
\omega=\sqrt{1+|\partial_{x}^{2}|}
above.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be an open interval,
T_{0}\in I
,

\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}
and
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{1}(I;\mathcal{E})
.
We say that
\boldsymbol{h}\in C(I;\mathcal{E})
is the solution of the Cauchy problem

(9.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t)=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}E_{0}(\boldsymbol{h}(t))+\boldsymbol{f}(t),

(9.5)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{h}(T_{0})=\boldsymbol{h}_{0}

if
\boldsymbol{h}
is given by the Duhamel formula

(9.6)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{h}(t)=\boldsymbol{S}_{0}(t)\boldsymbol{h}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\boldsymbol{S}_{0}(t-s)\boldsymbol{f}(s)\,\mathrm{\,d}s.\end{split}

Remark 9.2
.

It can be proved that the definition above is equivalent
to the notion of a weak solution, see
[
15
]

for more general results, but we will not need this fact.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be an open interval,
T_{0}\in I
,

\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}
,
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{1}(I;\mathcal{E})

and let
\boldsymbol{h}\in C(I;\mathcal{E})
be the solution of
(
9.4
)–(
9.5
).
For all
t\in I
the following
energy estimate
holds:

(9.7)

\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\bigg|\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{f}(s)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|.

Proof.

This follows from the Duhamel formula (
9.6
).
∎

In the remaining part of this section
I
will denote an open time interval and
(\vec{a},\vec{v})\in C^{1}(I;\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
are fixed
modulation parameters such that

(9.8)

\begin{split}\rho(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\leq\eta_{0}\quad\forall\,t\in I,\end{split}

where
\eta_{0}
is as in Proposition
2.14
.

Definition 9.4
.

Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be an open interval,
T_{0}\in I
,

\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}
and
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{1}(I;\mathcal{E})
.
We say that
\boldsymbol{h}\in C(I;\mathcal{E})
is the solution of the Cauchy problem

(9.9)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t)=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)+\boldsymbol{f}(t),

(9.10)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{h}(T_{0})=\boldsymbol{h}_{0}

if
\boldsymbol{h}
is the solution of (
9.4
)–(
9.5
) with
\boldsymbol{f}
replaced by

(9.11)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}(t)\coloneqq\boldsymbol{J}(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{0}(0))\boldsymbol{h}(t)+\boldsymbol{f}(t).

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be an open interval and
T_{0}\in I
.
For every
\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}
and
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{1}(I;\mathcal{E})

the problem (
9.9
)–(
9.10
)
has a unique solution
\boldsymbol{h}\in C(I;\mathcal{E})
.

If
\boldsymbol{\xi}\in C^{1}(I;L^{2}\times H^{1})
, then for all
t\in I

(9.12)

{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{t}}}\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle=\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\xi}(t)-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\xi}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}(t),\boldsymbol{f}(t)\rangle.

Proof.

We use the Picard iteration scheme.
We set
\boldsymbol{h}^{(0)}(t)\coloneqq 0
for all
t\in I
,
and define by induction
\boldsymbol{h}^{(n)}
for all
n\geq 1

as the solution of the equation

(9.13)

\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{(n)}(t)=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{0}(0)\boldsymbol{h}^{(n)}(t)+\boldsymbol{J}\big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{0}(0)\big)\boldsymbol{h}^{(n-1)}(t)+\boldsymbol{f}(t)

with the initial data
\boldsymbol{h}^{(n)}(T_{0})=\boldsymbol{h}_{0}
,
given by Proposition
9.3
.

Observing that
\boldsymbol{J}\big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{0}(0)\big)
is a uniformly (with respect to
t\in I
) bounded operator
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}

(in fact, it is even regularizing, with a gain of one derivative)
and applying (
9.7
), we obtain for all
t\in I

and
n\in\{1,\ldots\}

(9.14)

\|\boldsymbol{h}^{(n+1)}(t)-\boldsymbol{h}^{(n)}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq C\bigg|\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{(n)}(s)-\boldsymbol{h}^{(n-1)}(s)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|.

Hence, by straightforward induction,

(9.15)

\|\boldsymbol{h}^{(n+1)}(t)-\boldsymbol{h}^{(n)}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq\frac{C^{n}|t-T_{0}|^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}\bigg|\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{(1)}(s)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|,

which implies that
\boldsymbol{h}^{(n)}
converges in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(I;\mathcal{E})

to some
\boldsymbol{h}:I\to\mathcal{E}
.
By the continuity statement in Proposition
9.3
,
we obtain that
\boldsymbol{h}
is a solution of (
9.9
)–(
9.10
).

If
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{h}}
is another solution, then (
9.7
) yields

(9.16)

\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{h}}(t)-\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\leq C\bigg|\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{h}}(s)-\boldsymbol{h}(s)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|,

thus
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{h}}=\boldsymbol{h}
by the Gronwall’s inequality. The identity (
9.12
) follows using the Duhamel formula (
9.6
) for
\boldsymbol{h}(t)
together with an approximation argument.
∎

Remark 9.6
.

In order for (
9.12
) to hold, it would be sufficient
to assume that
\boldsymbol{\xi}\in C(I;L^{2}\times H^{1})

and
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\xi}\in C(I;H^{-1}\times L^{2})
.

Definition 9.7
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\in\{\mathcal{E}_{-,-},\mathcal{E}_{-,+},\mathcal{E}_{+,-},\mathcal{E}_{+,+}\}

and
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
.
We say that
\boldsymbol{\phi}:I\to\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
is a solution of (
1.10
) if
\boldsymbol{h}(t)\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)-\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}

is a solution of (
9.4
)–(
9.5
) with
\boldsymbol{h}_{0}=0
and

(9.17)

\boldsymbol{f}(t)\coloneqq\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}+\boldsymbol{h}(t))-\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}E_{0}(\boldsymbol{h}(t))

Proposition 9.8
.

For all
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
the problem
(
1.10
)
has a unique solution.
It is global in time and depends continuously on the initial data.

Proof.

This follows from the usual Picard iteration scheme.
∎

Recall the matrix
\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
defined in (
2.134
) and denote by
\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})^{\intercal}
its transpose. We recall again for convenience the unconventional notation

(9.18)

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}\vec{\mu}\\
\vec{\lambda}\end{pmatrix}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\lambda_{1}\\
\dots\\
\mu_{n}\\
\lambda_{n}\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

Lemma 9.9
.

Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be an open interval and
T_{0}\in I
.
For every
\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\in\mathcal{E}
such that

(9.19)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(T_{0}),\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(T_{0}),\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\rangle=0\qquad\text{for all }k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\end{split}

and
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{1}(I;\mathcal{E})

there exist unique
\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu}\in L^{1}(I;\mathbb{R}^{n})

such that the solution
\boldsymbol{h}\in C(I;\mathcal{E})
of the problem
(
9.9
)–(
9.10
)
with
\boldsymbol{f}(t)
replaced by

(9.20)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{f}(t)+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\lambda_{k}(t)\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu_{k}(t)\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t))\end{split}

satisfies for all
t\in I
and
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}

(9.21)

\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle=0.

Moreover, there is the relation

(9.22)

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}\vec{\mu}\\
\vec{\lambda}\end{pmatrix}=-\big[\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})^{-1}\big]^{\intercal}\begin{pmatrix}\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle\\
\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\rangle\\
\dots\\
\dots\\
\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}\rangle\\
\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}\rangle\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

Proof.

We follow the Picard iteration scheme.
We set
\vec{\lambda}^{(0)}(t)=\vec{\mu}^{(0)}(t)=0
for all
t\in I
,
and define inductively for all
\ell\geq 1
the function
\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell)}(t)

as the solution of the problem (
9.9
)–(
9.10
) with
\boldsymbol{f}(t)
replaced by

(9.23)

\boldsymbol{f}(t)+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(\lambda^{(\ell-1)}_{k}(t)\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu^{(\ell-1)}_{k}(t)\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)\big),

and

(\lambda^{(\ell)}(t),\vec{\mu}^{(\ell)}(t))
as the solution
of the linear system

(9.24)

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}\vec{\mu}^{(\ell)}(t)\\
\vec{\lambda}^{(\ell)}(t)\end{pmatrix}=-\big[\mathcal{M}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))^{-1}\big]^{\intercal}\begin{pmatrix}\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell)}(t),\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}(t)-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))J\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}(t)\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}(t)\rangle\\
\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell)}(t),\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}(t)-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))J\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}(t)\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}(t)\rangle\\
\dots\\
\dots\\
\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell)}(t),\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}(t)-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))J\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}(t)\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}(t)\rangle\\
\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell)}(t),\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}(t)-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))J\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}(t)\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}(t)\rangle\end{pmatrix}\end{split}

We then have

(9.25)

|\vec{\lambda}^{(\ell+1)}(t)-\vec{\lambda}^{(\ell)}(t)|+|\vec{\mu}^{(\ell+1)}(t)-\vec{\mu}^{(\ell)}(t)|\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell+1)}(t)-\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell)}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}

and

(9.26)

\|\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell+1)}(t)-\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell)}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim\bigg|\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\big(|\vec{\lambda}^{(\ell)}(s)-\vec{\lambda}^{(\ell-1)}(s)|+|\vec{\mu}^{(\ell)}(s)-\vec{\mu}^{(\ell-1)}(s)|\big)\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|.

We conclude similarly as in the proof of Lemma
9.5
above
that
(\vec{\lambda}^{(\ell)},\vec{\mu}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{h}^{(\ell)})
converge, yielding in particular the relationship (
9.22
). Using the identity (
9.12
) with
\boldsymbol{\xi}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}
and
\boldsymbol{f}
replaced by (
9.20
) we see that (
9.22
) ensures that

(9.27)

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{\,d}}{\mathrm{\,d}t}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t),\,\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle=\frac{\mathrm{\,d}}{\mathrm{\,d}t}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t),\,\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle=0\end{split}

for each
t\in I
and each
k
. Together with (
9.19
) the above gives (
9.21
).

In order to check the uniqueness of
(\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
, it suffices to prove that if
\boldsymbol{h}_{0}=0
,
\boldsymbol{f}=0

and
\boldsymbol{h}
satisfies (
9.21
) for all
t
, then
\vec{\lambda}=\vec{\mu}=0
.
From (
9.22
), we obtain

(9.28)

|\vec{\lambda}(t)|+|\vec{\mu}(t)|\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}

and from the estimate (
9.7
) and the fact that
\boldsymbol{J}\big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E_{0}(0)\big)
is a uniformly (with respect to
t\in I
) bounded operator
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
, we obtain

(9.29)

\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim\bigg|\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{h}(s)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+|\vec{\lambda}(s)|+|\vec{\mu}(s)|\big)\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|,

hence
\boldsymbol{h}(t)=0
and
\vec{\lambda}(t)=\vec{\mu}(t)=0
for all
t
by Gronwall’s inequality.
∎

We now turn to the question of various refined energy estimates. To motivate the estimates, we first examine the free evolution (
9.4
)–(
9.5
).

Remark 9.10
.

We differentiate a localised momentum for the free evolution
and see what we obtain. Let
\chi
be any function depending on
t
and
x
, and set

(9.30)

Q(t;\boldsymbol{h}_{1},\boldsymbol{h}_{2})\coloneqq{-}\frac{1}{2}\big(\big\langle\chi(t)\dot{h}_{2},\partial_{x}h_{1}\big\rangle+\big\langle\chi(t)\dot{h}_{1},\partial_{x}h_{2}\big\rangle\big).

Let
\boldsymbol{h}
be a solution of the problem (
9.4
)–(
9.5
).
We then obtain

(9.31)

\displaystyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{t}}}Q(t;\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t))

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}Q(t;\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t))

\displaystyle+2Q(t;\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{f}(t))

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\chi(t)(\partial_{x}^{2}h(t)-h(t))\partial_{x}h(t)+\chi(t)\dot{h}(t)\partial_{x}\dot{h}(t)\big)\mathrm{\,d}x

\displaystyle={-}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}\chi(t)\dot{h}(t)\partial_{x}h(t)\mathrm{\,d}x

\displaystyle{\displaystyle-}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\chi(t)\dot{f}(t)\partial_{x}h(t)+\chi(t)\dot{h}(t)\partial_{x}f(t)\big)\mathrm{\,d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}\chi(t)\big((\partial_{x}h(t))^{2}+(\dot{h}(t))^{2}-h(t)^{2}\big)\mathrm{\,d}x.

Next, we adapt the localized momentum to the linearization about a multikink, i.e., (
9.9
)–(
9.10
). For
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, let
\chi_{k}
be a bump function centered at
a_{k}(t)
, so that
\partial_{t}\chi_{k}
is of size
\lesssim|\vec{v}(t)|
and
\|\partial_{x}\chi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim y_{\mathrm{min}}(t)^{-1}
.
For all
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
t\in I
, we set

(9.32)

Q_{k}(t;\boldsymbol{h}_{1},\boldsymbol{h}_{2})\coloneqq{-}\frac{1}{2}\big(\big\langle\chi_{k}(t)\dot{h}_{2},\partial_{x}h_{1}\big\rangle+\big\langle\chi_{k}(t)\dot{h}_{1},\partial_{x}h_{2}\big\rangle\big),

which is a symmetric continuous bilinear function
\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 9.11
.

In the setting of Lemma
9.5
, we have

(9.33)

\displaystyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{t}}}Q_{k}(t;\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t))

\displaystyle=2Q_{k}(t;\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{f}(t))

\displaystyle{\displaystyle-}\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{D}^{3}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))(\chi_{k}(t)\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t))

\displaystyle+O((|\vec{v}(t)|+y_{\mathrm{min}}(t)^{-1})\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}).

Proof.

With respect to the computation in the Remark above, we have the additional term

(9.34)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{k}(t)U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))h(t)\partial_{x}h(t)\mathrm{\,d}x.

Integrating by parts, we get a negligible term and

(9.35)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{k}(t)\partial_{x}H(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))U^{\prime\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))h(t)^{2}\mathrm{\,d}x

\displaystyle={-}\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{D}^{3}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))(\chi_{k}(t)\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)).

as claimed.
∎

Lemma 9.12
.

In the setting of Lemma
9.5
, assume in addition that

(9.36)

\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(|\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle|^{2}+|\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle|^{2}\big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\delta(t),\qquad\text{for all }t\in I

for some continuous function
\delta:I\to[0,\infty)
. Then for all
t\in I

(9.37)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{h}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\exp\bigg(C\bigg|\int_{T_{0}}^{t}k(s)\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|\bigg)

\displaystyle+C\bigg|\int_{T_{0}}^{t}(\|\boldsymbol{f}(s)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\delta(s))\exp\Big(C\Big|\int_{s}^{t}k(s^{\prime})\mathrm{\,d}s^{\prime}\Big|\Big)\mathrm{\,d}s\bigg|,

where

(9.38)

k(t)\coloneqq|\vec{a}\,^{\prime}(t)-\vec{v}(t)|+|\vec{v}\,^{\prime}(t)|+|\vec{v}(t)|^{2}+|\vec{v}(t)|y_{\mathrm{min}}(t)^{-1}+y_{\mathrm{min}}(t)e^{-y_{\mathrm{min}}(t)}.

Proof of Lemma
9.12
.

First, observe that

(9.39)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{t}}}\frac{1}{2}\langle\boldsymbol{h}(t),\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{f}(t),\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{D}^{3}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\big(a_{k}^{\prime}(t)\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+v_{k}^{\prime}(t)\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)\big),\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\Big).

The most problematic term is the one containing
a_{k}^{\prime}(t)
.
Because of this term, we introduce a correction to the energy and introduce the following
modified energy functional
:

(9.40)

I(t)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\langle\boldsymbol{h}(t),\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle-\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}Q_{k}(t;\boldsymbol{h}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)),

where
Q_{k}
is defined by (
9.32
). Now observe that

(9.41)

\|\chi_{k}(t)\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+(-1)^{k}\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim e^{-cy_{\mathrm{min}}}\ll y_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1},

thus Lemma
9.11
yields

(9.42)

\displaystyle I^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{f}(t),\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle+O\big((|\vec{a}\,^{\prime}(t)-\vec{v}(t)|+|\vec{v}\,^{\prime}(t)|+|\vec{v}(t)|^{2}+|\vec{v}(t)|y_{\mathrm{min}}(t)^{-1})\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+O\big(|\vec{v}(t)|\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\big).

We set

(9.43)

\widetilde{I}(t)\coloneqq I(t)+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle^{2}+\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle^{2}\big).

By Proposition
2.14
, we have

(9.44)

\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}\lesssim\widetilde{I}(t).

Using the computation above and (
2.32
), (
2.33
), we obtain

(9.45)

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{f}(t),\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle+O\big(k(t)\widetilde{I}(t)+\delta(t)\sqrt{\widetilde{I}(t)}\big),

We thus have

(9.46)

|\widetilde{I}^{\prime}(t)|\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\widetilde{I}(t)}+k(t)\widetilde{I}(t)\big),

and the conclusion follows from Gronwall’s inequality, see
[
2
, Lemma 3.3]
.
∎

10.
Analysis of the projected equation

In this section we study the so-called projected equation in the Lyapunov-Schmidt scheme. This amounts to fixing a pair of trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
within a certain function class.
The trajectories that we consider satisfy the following conditions.
We set

(10.1)

t_{0}\coloneqq\exp\big(y_{\mathrm{min}}(0)/2\big).

We then require that for all
t\geq 0

(10.2)

|\vec{a}\,^{\prime}(t)-\vec{v}(t)|+|\vec{v}\,^{\prime}(t)|+|\vec{v}(t)|^{2}+\exp({-}y_{\mathrm{min}}(t))\lesssim(t_{0}+t)^{-2},

with a constant independent of
y_{\mathrm{min}}(0)
.

Given
\gamma>0
and a normed space
E
, we define the norm
\|\cdot\|_{N_{\gamma}(E)}

on the space of continuous functions
\boldsymbol{f}:[0,\infty)\to E
by the formula

(10.3)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{\gamma}(E)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{t\geq 0}\big[(t_{0}+t)^{\gamma}\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{E}\big].

We write
N_{\gamma}
instead of
N_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R})
.

The first three lemmas below concern the linear equation

(10.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)+\boldsymbol{f}(t)+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{k}(t)\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu_{k}(t)\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t),

with
\boldsymbol{f}\in N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})
for some
\gamma>0
and trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
as in (
10.2
).

Lemma 10.1
.

For all
\nu>0
there exists
C>0
such that the following is true.
Let
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
be a solution of (
10.4
) on the time interval
I=[T,\infty)

such that
\boldsymbol{h}\in N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})
for some
\gamma>0
and some
T\geq 0
.
Assume in addition that

(10.5)

\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}|\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle|^{2}+|\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\delta(t),\qquad\text{for all }t\in I

for some continuous function
\delta:I\to[0,\infty)
. Then

(10.6)

\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{\nu}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{\nu+1}(\mathcal{E})}+\|\delta\|_{N_{\nu+1}}).

Proof.

We apply Lemma
9.12
with
\boldsymbol{h}_{0}=\boldsymbol{0}
and let
T_{0}\to\infty
.
Observe that for fixed
t
and all
T_{0}\geq t
we have, with
\epsilon
arbitrarily small upon taking
\tau_{0}
large enough,

(10.7)

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T_{0}}(\|\boldsymbol{f}(s)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\delta(s))\exp\bigg(C\int_{t}^{s}k(s^{\prime})\mathrm{\,d}s^{\prime}\bigg)\mathrm{\,d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{\nu+1}(\mathcal{E})}+\|\delta\|_{N_{\nu+1}})\int_{t}^{T_{0}}(t_{0}+s)^{-\nu-1}\exp\bigg(\epsilon\int_{t}^{s}\frac{1}{t_{0}+s^{\prime}}\mathrm{\,d}s^{\prime}\bigg)\mathrm{\,d}s

\displaystyle=(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{\nu+1}(\mathcal{E})}+\|\delta\|_{N_{\nu+1}})(t_{0}+t)^{-\epsilon}\int_{t}^{T_{0}}(t_{0}+s)^{-\nu+\epsilon-1}\mathrm{\,d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim(t_{0}+t)^{-\nu}(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{\nu+1}(\mathcal{E})}+\|\delta\|_{N_{\nu+1}}),

which gives the claimed bound.
∎

Remark 10.2
.

We could perhaps obtain
\|\delta\|_{N_{\nu+0.5}}
instead of
\|\delta\|_{N_{\nu+1}}
on the right hand side, if we perform differently the energy estimate.

Lemma 10.3
.

For any
\gamma>0
there exists
\tau_{0}
such that if
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
, then
for any
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
as in (
10.2
) and
\boldsymbol{f}\in N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})

there exists a unique solution
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
of the equation (
10.4
)
such that
\boldsymbol{h}\in N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})
and (
9.21
) holds for all
t\geq 0
.

Moreover,
\boldsymbol{f}\mapsto\boldsymbol{h}
is a bounded linear operator
N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})\to N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})

(uniformly with respect to
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
).

Finally, for all
t\geq 0
and all
\epsilon>0
we have

(10.8)

\displaystyle|M\lambda_{k}(t)+\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{f}(t)\rangle|\leq C_{\epsilon}(t_{0}+t)^{-2+\epsilon}(\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}),

\displaystyle|M\mu_{k}(t)-\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{f}(t)\rangle|\leq C_{\epsilon}(t_{0}+t)^{-2+\epsilon}(\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}).

In one instance we require a subtle refinement of Lemma
10.3
under decay assumptions on
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{f}
. This trick was pointed out to us by Yvan Martel.

Lemma 10.4
.

In the setting of Lemma
10.3
, suppose in addition that
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{f}\in N_{\gamma+2}(\mathcal{E})
. Then,

\boldsymbol{h}
satisfies the estimates

(10.9)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}\Big(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}+\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{\gamma+2}(\mathcal{E})}\Big).\end{split}

Proof of Lemma
10.3
.

Fixing
\gamma>0
, we have
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{1}([t_{0},\infty);\mathcal{E})\subset N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})
. Then, letting
\boldsymbol{h}_{0}=\boldsymbol{0}
and
T_{0}\to\infty
in Lemma
9.9
we obtain a unique triplet
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})\in C([t_{0},\infty);\mathcal{E})\times L^{1}([t_{0},\infty))\times L^{1}([t_{0},\infty))
solving (
10.4
) and such that (
9.21
) holds. Using Lemma
9.12
and arguing as in Lemma
10.1
we obtain the estimate

(10.10)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}.\end{split}

We next prove (
10.8
). In light of (
2.28
), (
2.32
), and (
2.33
) we note the identities

(10.11)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}&=(a_{k}^{\prime}-v_{k})\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+v_{k}^{\prime}\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\\
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}&=(a_{k}^{\prime}-v_{k})\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}+v^{\prime}_{k}\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}.\end{split}

Starting with the expression (
9.22
), using the boundedness of
\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})^{-1}
for trajectories as in (
10.2
), the above with (
9.21
), and (
10.2
) we obtain the preliminary estimates

(10.12)

\begin{split}|\vec{\lambda}(t)|+|\vec{\mu}(t)|\lesssim(t+t_{0})^{-2}\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}.\end{split}

To obtain (
10.8
) we pair the equation (
10.4
) with
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}
and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}
yielding

(10.13)

\begin{split}\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle+\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\langle\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{j},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{j}\langle\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{j},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\\
\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle+\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\langle\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{j},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{j}\langle\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{j},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle.\end{split}

Using (
2.30
) (
2.29
) and (
9.27
) we obtain the expressions

(10.14)

\begin{split}M\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}\lambda_{k}+\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle&=-\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle+\sum_{j\neq k}\lambda_{j}\langle\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{j},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{j}\langle\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{j},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\\
&\quad-\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\Big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\Big)\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle\\
M\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}\mu_{k}-\langle\boldsymbol{f},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle-\sum_{j\neq k}\lambda_{j}\langle\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{j},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle+\mu_{j}\langle\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{j},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle\\
&\quad+\langle\boldsymbol{h},\,\Big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\Big)\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle.\end{split}

Noting that
\gamma_{v_{k}}^{-3}-1=O((t_{0}+t)^{-2})
, using (
10.11
) and (
10.2
) on the first terms on the right, Lemma
2.3
to estimate the cross terms in the sum over
j\neq k
, and (
2.66
) for the last terms in each expression completes the proof of (
10.8
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
10.4
.

We introduce another modified energy functional. Letting
I(t)
be as in the proof of Lemma
9.12
we set

(10.15)

\begin{split}\check{I}(t)\coloneqq I(t)+\langle\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{h}(t),\,\boldsymbol{f}(t)\rangle\end{split}

By Proposition
2.14
we have

(10.16)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}\lesssim\check{I}(t)+\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\end{split}

Using (
9.42
), and recalling that
\boldsymbol{h}(t)
satisfies the equation

(10.17)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{f}+\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}+\mu_{k}\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\end{split}

we compute

(10.18)

\begin{split}\check{I}^{\prime}(t)&=I^{\prime}(t)+\langle\boldsymbol{J}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t),\,\boldsymbol{f}(t)\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{h}(t),\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{f}(t)\rangle\\
&=\langle\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{h},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{f}\rangle+\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{f}\rangle+\mu_{k}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{f}\rangle\\
&\quad+O\big((|\vec{a}\,^{\prime}-\vec{v}|+|\vec{v}\,^{\prime}|+|\vec{v}|^{2}+|\vec{v}|y_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1})\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}\big)+O\big(|\vec{v}|\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\big).\end{split}

Using (
10.8
) and the estimates (
10.12
) we note the cancellation

(10.19)

\begin{split}|\lambda_{k}\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{f}\rangle+\mu_{k}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{f}\rangle|&=M^{-1}\big|\lambda_{k}(\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{f}\rangle-M\mu_{k})+\mu_{k}(\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\,\boldsymbol{f}\rangle+M\lambda_{k})\Big|\\
&\lesssim(t_{0}+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}})\Big((t+t_{0})^{-2}\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\Big)\\
&\lesssim(t_{0}+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}).\end{split}

Using this in the previous line we are led to the estimate

(10.20)

\begin{split}|\check{I}^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim\frac{1}{(t_{0}+t)\log t}\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}+\frac{1}{t_{0}+t}\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\frac{1}{(t_{0}+t)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{h}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\end{split}

and the conclusion follows by integration.
∎

Next, we consider the nonlinear problem for a fixed trajectory
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
as in (
10.2
).

Lemma 10.5

(The nonlinear equation)
.

There exist
\delta,\tau_{0}>0
such that for any
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}

and
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
satisfying (
10.2
) there exists a unique solution
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
of the equation

(10.21)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t)&=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\boldsymbol{h}(t))+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(\lambda_{k}(t)\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu_{k}(t)\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)\big)\end{split}

such that
\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{1}}\leq\delta
and (
9.21
) holds for all
t\geq 0
.
This solution satisfies
\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{\gamma}}\leq C_{\gamma}
and

(10.22)

\begin{split}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\|\lambda_{k}(t)+(-1)^{k}v_{k}(t)\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}&\leq C_{\gamma}\\
\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\|M\mu_{k}(t)+(-1)^{k}F_{k}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}&\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

for any
\gamma<2
, where
F_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})
is defined in (
2.41
).

Before we give a proof (which is based on the Contraction Principle),
we need one more auxiliary result, which provides estimates on the first step
of the Picard iteration. It relies on an explicit ansatz and results in a better bound
than the one obtained from a direct application of the energy estimates.

Lemma 10.6
.

Let
(\boldsymbol{h}_{I},\vec{\lambda}_{I},\vec{\mu}_{I})
be the solution of

(10.23)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{I}(t)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}_{I}(t)+\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(\lambda_{I,k}(t)\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu_{I,k}(t)\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)\big)

given by Lemma
10.3
. For each
0<\gamma<2
there exists a constant
C_{\gamma}>0
so that
(\boldsymbol{h}_{I},\vec{\lambda}_{I},\vec{\mu}_{I})
satisfy
\|\boldsymbol{h}_{I}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}
, and

(10.24)

\begin{split}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\Big(\|\lambda_{I,k}(t)+(-1)^{k}v_{k}(t)\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}+\|M\mu_{I,k}(t)+(-1)^{k}F_{k}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\Big)&\leq C_{\gamma}.\end{split}

Proof.

Let
(\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(t),\vec{\lambda}_{S}(t),\vec{\mu}_{S}(t))
be the solution given by Lemma
2.15
part
1
of

(10.25)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(t)&=-\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{S,k}(t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t)+\mu_{S,k}(t)\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t))\\
&=\boldsymbol{f}_{S}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big((\lambda_{S,k}(t)+(-1)^{k}v_{k}(t))\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t)+\mu_{S,k}(t)\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t)\big)\end{split}

with

(10.26)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{S},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{S},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle=0,\end{split}

for all
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
and

(10.27)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{f}_{S}=-\begin{pmatrix}U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}U^{\prime}(H_{k})\\
0\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

By (
2.140
), and Lemma
2.6
we have

(10.28)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{f}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim\|U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}U^{\prime}(H_{k})\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sqrt{y_{\min}(t)}e^{-y_{\min}(t)}\end{split}

which, together with (
10.2
) gives

(10.29)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}_{S}\|_{N_{\gamma}}\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

for any
\gamma<2
.
By (
2.27
) along with Lemma
2.6
we have, for each
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}

(10.30)

\begin{split}|\langle\partial_{v_{k}}H_{k}(t),\,U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}U^{\prime}(H_{k})\rangle|\lesssim|v_{k}(t)|\sqrt{y_{\min}(t)}e^{-y_{\min}(t)}\end{split}

It then follows from (
2.141
) and the above that

(10.31)

\begin{split}|\lambda_{S,k}(t)+(-1)^{k}v_{k}(t)|&\lesssim|v_{k}(t)|\sqrt{y_{\min}(t)}e^{-y_{\min}(t)}\\
&\quad+(\sqrt{y_{\min}(t)}e^{-y_{\min}(t)}+|\vec{v}|)\|\boldsymbol{h}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\\
&\quad+y_{\min}(t)e^{-y_{\min}(t)}\|U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}U^{\prime}(H_{k})\|_{\mathcal{E}}\end{split}

Using (
10.2
), Lemma
2.6
and (
10.2
) we see that for any
\gamma<2

(10.32)

\begin{split}\|\lambda_{S,k}(t)+(-1)^{k}v_{k}(t)\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

Using the same logic, we arrive at the bound

(10.33)

\begin{split}|M\gamma_{v_{k}(t)}^{3}\mu_{S,k}(t)-\langle\partial_{a_{k}}H_{k}(t),\,U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}U^{\prime}(H_{k})\rangle|\leq C_{\gamma}t^{-\gamma-1}\end{split}

or equivalently,

(10.34)

\begin{split}\|M\mu_{S,k}(t)+(-1)^{k}F_{k}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

where we have used above that
|\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}-1|\lesssim(t_{0}+t)^{-2}
.

We will also require bounds for

(10.35)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(t)=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))=\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}^{\prime}(t)(\partial_{a_{j}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S})(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+v_{j}^{\prime}(t)(\partial_{v_{j}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S})(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\end{split}

Using (
2.163
) from Lemma
2.17
along with (
2.65
) and (
2.66
) from Lemma
2.6
we note the estimates,

(10.36)

\begin{split}\|\partial_{a_{j}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\|_{\mathcal{E}}&\lesssim\sqrt{y_{\min}(t)}e^{-y_{\min}(t)}\\
\|\partial_{v_{j}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\|_{\mathcal{E}}&\lesssim\sqrt{y_{\min}(t)}e^{-y_{\min}(t)}\end{split}

and by the same token, using now (
2.164
) from Lemma
2.17
we have

(10.37)

\begin{split}\|\partial_{a_{j}}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\partial_{v_{j}}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\|_{\mathcal{E}}&\lesssim\sqrt{y_{\min}(t)}e^{-y_{\min}(t)}\end{split}

which we record for later.
Together with (
10.2
) it follows that

(10.38)

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

for any
\gamma<2
.

We write
\boldsymbol{h}_{I}=\boldsymbol{h}_{S}+\boldsymbol{h}_{R}
,
\vec{\lambda}_{I}=\vec{\lambda}_{S}+\vec{\lambda}_{R}
, and
\vec{\mu}_{I}=\vec{\mu}_{S}+\vec{\mu}_{R}
. Then (
10.23
) is equivalent to
(\boldsymbol{h}_{R},\vec{\lambda}_{R},\vec{\mu}_{R})
solving

(10.39)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{R}(t)&=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}_{R}(t)-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(t)\\
&\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{R,k}(t)\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu_{R,k}(t)\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)\end{split}

and

(10.40)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{R}(t),\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t)\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{R}(t),\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t)\rangle=0,\end{split}

for all
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.
From Lemma
10.3
and (
10.38
) we have

(10.41)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}_{R}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

and

(10.42)

\begin{split}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\big(\|\lambda_{R,k}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}+\|\mu_{R,k}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\big)\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

for any
\gamma<2
as claimed. Combining the above with (
10.29
), (
10.32
), and (
10.34
) gives the lemma.

We also require an improvement over the estimate (
10.41
). We claim that in fact

(10.43)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}_{R}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

for all
\gamma<2
. To see this we rewrite the forcing term
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}
in the equation for
{\boldsymbol{h}}_{R}
as

(10.44)

\begin{split}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}(t)&=\boldsymbol{f}_{R,1}(t)+\boldsymbol{f}_{R,2}(t),\end{split}

where

(10.45)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{f}_{R,1}(t)\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big((a_{k}^{\prime}(t)-v_{k}(t)\Big)(\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S})(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}^{\prime}(t)(\partial_{v_{j}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S})(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)),\end{split}

and

(10.46)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{f}_{R,2}(t)\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}(t)(\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S})(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)).\end{split}

We then let
(\boldsymbol{h}_{R,j},\vec{\lambda}_{R,j},\vec{\mu}_{R,j})
for
j=1,2
be the solution to

(10.47)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{R,j}=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}_{R,j}+\boldsymbol{f}_{R,j}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{R,j,k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}+\mu_{R,j,k}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\end{split}

and

(10.48)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{R,j},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{R,j},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle=0,\end{split}

for all
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. An application of Lemma
10.3
for the above equation with
j=1
yields the bound

(10.49)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}_{R,1}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}\|\boldsymbol{f}_{R,1}\|_{N_{\gamma+2}}\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

which, using (
10.2
), holds for all
\gamma<2
. To prepare for an application of Lemma
10.4
to treat the case
j=2
we first compute

(10.50)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{R,2}(t)&=\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}^{\prime}(t)(\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S})(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}v_{k}(t)a_{j}^{\prime}(t)(\partial_{a_{j}}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S})(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\\
&\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}(t)v_{j}^{\prime}(t)(\partial_{v_{j}}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S})(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)).\end{split}

Using (
10.36
), (
10.37
) and (
10.2
) we see that in fact

(10.51)

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{f}_{R,2}\|_{N_{\gamma+2}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma},\end{split}

for any
\gamma<2
and hence by Lemma
10.4
we have

(10.52)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}_{R,2}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}\end{split}

for any
\gamma<2
. This proves (
10.43
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
10.5
.

We divide the proof into five steps.

Step 1.
(Formulation as a fixed point problem.)
Given
\boldsymbol{g}\in N_{1}(\mathcal{E})
such that
\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{N_{1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq\delta
, let
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
be the unique solution of

(10.53)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)+J\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))

\displaystyle+\boldsymbol{J}\big(\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\boldsymbol{g}(t))-\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{g}(t)\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{k}(t)\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu_{k}(t)\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)

given by Lemma
10.3
, which applies here since

(10.54)

\|\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\boldsymbol{g}(t))-\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{g}(t)\|_{N_{2}}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{N_{1}}^{2}.

We set
\Phi(\boldsymbol{g})\coloneqq\boldsymbol{h}
, so that
\boldsymbol{h}
solves (
10.21
) if and only if
\Phi(\boldsymbol{h})=\boldsymbol{h}
.

Step 2.
(Estimates on
\Phi(\boldsymbol{0})
.) The so-called first Picard iterate is given by
\boldsymbol{h}_{I}\coloneqq\Phi(\boldsymbol{0})
where
\boldsymbol{h}_{I}
is as in Lemma
10.6
and the equation for
\boldsymbol{h}_{I}
is (
10.23
). By Lemma
10.6
we have

(10.55)

\|\Phi(\boldsymbol{0})\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma},\qquad\text{for all }\gamma<2,

and thus
\|\Phi(\boldsymbol{0})\|_{N_{1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}
provided
\tau_{0}
is chosen large enough.

Step 3.
(Estimates on
\Phi(\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp})-\Phi(\boldsymbol{g})
.)
Let
\boldsymbol{g},\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}\in N_{1}(\mathcal{E})
and
\max(\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{N_{1}(\mathcal{E})},\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}\|_{N_{1}(\mathcal{E})})\leq\delta
.
Set

(10.56)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\boldsymbol{J}\big(\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+{\boldsymbol{g}}(t))-\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))){\boldsymbol{g}}(t)\big)

\displaystyle=\big(0,{-}U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+g(t))+U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+U^{\prime\prime}(H(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))g(t)\big),

and define
\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}(t)
by the same formula, with
\boldsymbol{g}
replaced by
\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}
.
Then
\Phi(\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp})-\Phi(\boldsymbol{g})=\boldsymbol{h}
, where
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})

is the solution of

(10.57)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t)

\displaystyle=J\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)+\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}(t)-\boldsymbol{f}(t)

\displaystyle+\vec{\lambda}(t)\cdot\partial_{\vec{a}}\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\vec{\mu}(t)\cdot\partial_{\vec{v}}\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))

Applying (
2.108
) with
w=w^{\sharp}=H(\vec{a},\vec{v})
, we obtain

(10.58)

\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}(t)-\boldsymbol{f}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim(\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{g}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}})\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}(t)-\boldsymbol{g}(t)\|_{\mathcal{E}},

hence

(10.59)

\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{f}\|_{N_{2}}\lesssim(\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}\|_{N_{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{N_{1}})\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{g}\|_{N_{1}}.

It thus follows from Lemma
10.3
that

(10.60)

\|\Phi(\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp})-\Phi(\boldsymbol{g})\|_{N_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{g}\|_{N_{1}}.

if
\delta
is small enough. Once
\delta
is fixed, we choose
t_{0}
large enough, so that
Step 2 yields
\|\Phi(\boldsymbol{0})\|_{N_{1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}
.
Then
\Phi
is a strict contraction on the closed ball of radius
\delta
in
N_{1}(\mathcal{E})
and the Contraction Mapping Principle yields a unique fixed point.

Step 4.
(Improved time decay of
\boldsymbol{h}(t)
.)
Fix any
\gamma\in(1,2)
. Because of (
10.55
) the closed unit ball in
N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})
is invariant under
\Phi
for sufficiently large
\tau_{0}
and by the same argument as above we see that
\Phi
is a strict contraction on the closed unit ball in
N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})
, based on the estimate

(10.61)

\begin{split}\|\Phi(\boldsymbol{g})-\Phi(\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp})\|_{N_{2\gamma-1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}+\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})})\|\boldsymbol{g}-\boldsymbol{g}^{\sharp}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}.\end{split}

By the Contraction Mapping Principle there is a unique fixed point in the unit ball in
N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})
, but since
N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})\subset N_{1}(\mathcal{E})
we deduce that the unique fixed point in the
\delta
-ball in
N_{1}(\mathcal{E})
belongs to
N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})
for all
1<\gamma<2
.

Step 5.
(Estimates on the parameters) Let
\boldsymbol{h}
denote the solution to (
10.21
) found above.

Denote
\boldsymbol{h}_{I}\coloneqq\Phi(0)
, let
\boldsymbol{h}
be the fixed point of
\Phi
found above and let
\boldsymbol{h}_{*}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{h}-\boldsymbol{h}_{I}
.
By the same argument as in Step 4, we have

(10.62)

\|\Phi(\boldsymbol{h}_{I}+\boldsymbol{h}_{*})-\Phi(\boldsymbol{0})\|_{N_{2\gamma-1}(\mathcal{E})}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{h}_{I}+\boldsymbol{h}_{*}\|_{N_{\gamma}}^{2}.

But
\Phi(\boldsymbol{h}_{I}+\boldsymbol{h}_{*})-\Phi(0)=\boldsymbol{h}-\boldsymbol{h}_{I}=\boldsymbol{h}_{*}
, hence we obtain

(10.63)

\|\boldsymbol{h}_{*}\|_{N_{\gamma}}\leq C_{\gamma},\qquad\text{for all }\gamma<3.

Similarly, write
\vec{\lambda}=\vec{\lambda}_{I}+\vec{\lambda}_{*}
, and
\vec{\mu}=\vec{\mu}_{I}+\vec{\mu}_{*}
, where
(\vec{\lambda}_{I},\vec{\mu}_{I})
are as in Lemma
10.6
. We see that
(\boldsymbol{h}_{*},\vec{\lambda}_{*},\vec{\mu}_{*})
solve

(10.64)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{*}(t)&=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}_{*}(t)\\
&+\boldsymbol{J}\big(\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\boldsymbol{h}(t))-\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}(t)\big)\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{*,k}(t)\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu_{*,k}(t)\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t).\end{split}

By Lemma
10.3
, (
10.63
), and that
\boldsymbol{h}\in N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})
for
\gamma<2
we see that

(10.65)

\|\vec{\lambda}_{*}\|_{N_{\gamma}}+\|\vec{\mu}_{*}\|_{N_{\gamma}}\leq C_{\gamma},\qquad\text{for all }\gamma<4.

Then (
10.22
) follow from the above and Lemma
10.6
.
∎

Finally, we study the dependence of the solution
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})

obtained in Lemma
10.5
on the choice of
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
.
Again, we begin by studying the first step of the Picard iteration.

Lemma 10.7
.

Let
(\boldsymbol{h}_{I},\vec{\lambda}_{I},\vec{\mu}_{I})
be the solution of (
10.23
)
given by Lemma
10.3

and
(\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{I},\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp}_{I},\vec{\mu}^{\sharp}_{I})
the solution of the same equation with
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
replaced by

(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})
. Then for all
\gamma<2
there exists
C_{\gamma}>0
such that

(10.66)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{I}-\boldsymbol{h}_{I}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}&\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}),\end{split}

(10.67)

\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\|(\lambda^{\sharp}_{I,k}-\lambda_{I,k})+(-1)^{k}(v^{\sharp}_{k}-v_{k})\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\\
\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}),

and,

(10.68)

\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\|(\mu^{\sharp}_{I,k}-\mu_{I,k})+(-1)^{k}(M^{-1}F_{k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})-M^{-1}F_{k}({\vec{a}},{\vec{v}}))\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\\
\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}).

Proof.

Following the main idea in the proof of Lemma
10.6
we will use an ansatz to extract and estimate the main terms. We decompose
\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{I}-{\boldsymbol{h}}_{I}=\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}+{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R}
,
\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp}_{I}-{\vec{\lambda}}_{I}=\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}_{S}+\vec{\lambda}_{R}^{\flat}
, and
\vec{\mu}^{\sharp}_{I}-{\vec{\mu}}_{I}=\vec{\mu}^{\flat}_{S}+\vec{\mu}^{\flat}_{R}
, where
\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}=\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{S}-\boldsymbol{h}_{S}
,
{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R}=\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{R}-\boldsymbol{h}_{R}
,
\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}_{S}=\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp}_{S}-\vec{\lambda}_{S}
,
\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}_{R}=\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp}_{R}-\vec{\lambda}_{R}
,
\vec{\mu}^{\flat}_{S}=\vec{\mu}^{\sharp}_{S}-\vec{\mu}_{S}
, and
\vec{\mu}^{\flat}_{R}=\vec{\mu}^{\sharp}_{R}-\vec{\mu}_{R}
.

It follows that
(\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S},\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}_{S},\vec{\mu}^{\flat}_{S})
solves

(10.69)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}&=\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}_{S}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(\lambda^{\flat}_{S,k}+(-1)^{k}(v^{\sharp}_{k}-v_{k}))\big)\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu^{\flat}_{S,k}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k},\end{split}

with

(10.70)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{S},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle=\ell_{k}\\
\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle&=\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{S},\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle=m_{k},\end{split}

and

(10.71)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}_{S}&=-\big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}({\vec{a}},{\vec{v}}))\big)\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{S}\\
&\quad-\begin{pmatrix}U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}U^{\prime}(H^{\sharp}_{k})\\
0\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}U^{\prime}(H(\vec{a},\vec{v}))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}U^{\prime}(H_{k})\\
0\end{pmatrix}\\
&\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\lambda^{\sharp}_{S,k}+(-1)^{k}v^{\sharp}_{k})\big(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\big)+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu^{\sharp}_{S,k}\big(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\big),\end{split}

where we have used the notation
H^{\sharp}_{k}=H(a^{\sharp}_{k},v^{\sharp}_{k}),\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}=\boldsymbol{J}\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}(a^{\sharp}_{k},\beta^{\sharp}_{k})
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}=\boldsymbol{J}\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}(a^{\sharp}_{k},\beta^{\sharp}_{k})
.

From Lemma
2.17
(
2.165
) we obtain the estimates

(10.72)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\lesssim\big(|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|+|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|\big)\big(\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\big)+\|\boldsymbol{f}_{S}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{f}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}},\end{split}

where
\boldsymbol{f}_{S},\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}
are as in (
10.27
).
For each
\gamma<2
it follows, using (
10.28
) that

(10.73)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}\big(\|\vec{a}-\vec{a}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}-\vec{v}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big).\end{split}

We next use (
2.166
), which gives

(10.74)

\begin{split}|\lambda^{\flat}_{S,k}&+(-1)^{k}(v^{\sharp}_{k}-v_{k})|\lesssim\big((\gamma_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}\rangle-(\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\boldsymbol{f}_{S}\rangle\big)\big|\\
&\quad+|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}})(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}})\\
&\quad+|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}})+C\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}-\boldsymbol{f}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}),\end{split}

from which we deduce that

(10.75)

\begin{split}\|\lambda^{\flat}_{S,k}&+(-1)^{k}(v^{\sharp}_{k}-v_{k})\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}\big(\|\vec{a}-\vec{a}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}-\vec{v}^{\sharp}\|_{N_{1}}\big).\end{split}

for each
\gamma<2
.
Similarly, from (
2.167
) we obtain

(10.76)

|\mu^{\flat}_{S,k}+(-1)^{k}((\gamma_{v^{\sharp}_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}F_{k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})-(\gamma_{v_{k}}^{3}M)^{-1}F_{k}({\vec{a}},{\vec{v}}))|\\
\lesssim|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}|(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}})(\|\boldsymbol{f}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}})\\
+|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}|(\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}})+C\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\sharp}_{S}-\boldsymbol{f}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}(|\vec{v}^{\sharp}|+|\vec{v}|+y_{\min}e^{-y_{\min}}),

from which we see that

(10.77)

\begin{split}\|\mu^{\flat}_{S,k}+(-1)^{k}(M^{-1}F_{k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})-M^{-1}F_{k}({\vec{a}},{\vec{v}}))\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}\big(\|\vec{a}-\vec{a}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}-\vec{v}^{\sharp}\|_{N_{1}}\big),\end{split}

for each
\gamma<2
.

Next, we consider the equation for
\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R}
, which is

(10.78)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R}&=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v})){\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R}+{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\flat}_{R}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda^{\flat}_{R,k}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}+\mu^{\flat}_{R,k}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k},\end{split}

where

(10.79)

\begin{split}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\flat}_{R}&\coloneqq-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}+\boldsymbol{J}\big((\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}({\vec{a}},{\vec{v}}))){\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R}\big)\\
&\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda^{\sharp}_{R,k}\big(\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\big)-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu^{\sharp}_{R,k}\big(\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}-\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\big),\end{split}

and

(10.80)

\begin{split}\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle&=\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R},\,{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle\\
\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle&=\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R},\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle.\end{split}

Define

(10.81)

\begin{split}\delta^{\flat}(t)\coloneqq\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R},\,{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle)^{2}+(\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R},\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

Using (
10.43
) we have

(10.82)

\begin{split}\|\delta^{\flat}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}),\end{split}

for all
\gamma<2
. We claim that

(10.83)

\begin{split}\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\flat}_{R}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}})\end{split}

for all
\gamma<2
. Using Lemma
10.1
and the previous two lines we obtain the estimates

(10.84)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R}\|_{N_{\gamma}}\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}),\end{split}

for all
\gamma<2
.

We prove (
10.83
). We have

(10.85)

\begin{split}\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\flat}_{R}\|_{\mathcal{E}}&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\|\boldsymbol{J}\big((\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}({\vec{a}},{\vec{v}}))){\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R}\big)\|_{\mathcal{E}}\\
&\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n}|\lambda^{\sharp}_{R,k}|\|\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\|_{\mathcal{E}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}|\mu^{\sharp}_{R,k}|\|\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}-\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\|_{\mathcal{E}}.\end{split}

We use the refined estimate (
10.43
) to estimate the second term by

(10.86)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{J}\big((\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}({\vec{a}},{\vec{v}})))\widetilde{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}\big)\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}),\end{split}

for any
\gamma<2
. The same bound for the third and fourth terms follows from (
10.24
). We claim that
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}(t)
satisfies

(10.87)

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}),\end{split}

for all
\gamma<2
. To see this, we write

(10.88)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{S}&=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{S}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big((a^{\sharp}_{k})^{\prime}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{S}-a_{k}^{\prime}\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}\big)+\big((v^{\sharp}_{k})^{\prime}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{S}-v_{k}^{\prime}\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{h}_{S}\big),\end{split}

and the bound (
10.87
) follows using (
2.168
) from Lemma
2.17
. This completes the proof of (
10.83
) and thus of (
10.84
).

We turn to the estimates for the parameters. We claim that

(10.89)

\|\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}_{R}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}+\|\vec{\mu}^{\flat}_{R}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\\
\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}})

for all
\gamma<2
. Arguing as usual, i.e., multiplying the equation (
10.78
) by
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}
,
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}
, we arrive at the following expression for the parameters

(10.90)

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}\vec{\mu}^{\flat}_{R}\\
\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}_{R}\end{pmatrix}=-\big[\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})^{-1}\big]^{\intercal}\begin{pmatrix}-\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}\rangle\\
-\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}\rangle\\
\dots\\
\dots\\
-\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{n}\rangle\\
-\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}\rangle\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

First, we show that for each
k

(10.91)

\|\langle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}+\|\langle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\\
\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}),

for each
\gamma<2
.
To see the above we differentiate (
10.80
) to obtain the identities

(10.92)

\begin{split}\langle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle&=-\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle+\langle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R},\,{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle+\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R},\,\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{k}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle,\\
\langle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle&=-\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle+\langle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R},\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle+\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\sharp}_{R},\,\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle.\end{split}

We consider only the second line above as the treatment of the first line is analogous. First,

(10.93)

\begin{split}|\langle{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R},\,\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle|\lesssim\|{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\flat}_{R}\|_{\mathcal{E}}\big(|\vec{a}^{\prime}|+|\vec{v}^{\prime}|\big),\end{split}

which is bounded by the right-hand side of (
10.91
) by (
10.84
) along with (
10.2
).
For the second term on the right of the second line we use (
10.39
), giving

(10.94)

\begin{split}\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{R},\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle&=-\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{R},\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))\boldsymbol{J}(\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k})\rangle-\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{S},\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle\\
&\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda^{\sharp}_{R,k}\langle\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k},\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle+\mu^{\sharp}_{R,k}\langle\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k},\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle.\end{split}

The last three terms on the right are bounded by the right-hand side of (
10.91
), using (
10.38
) and (
10.42
). The first term on the right can be written, using (
2.33
) as

(10.95)

\begin{split}-&\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{R},\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))\boldsymbol{J}(\,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k})\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{R},\,v^{\sharp}_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}-v_{k}\partial_{x}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\\
&\quad+\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{R},\,\big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp}))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k}^{\sharp})\big)\big(\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}-\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}\big)\rangle\\
&\quad+\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{R},\big(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k}^{\sharp})-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}_{k})\big)\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}\rangle,\end{split}

and the terms on the right are all bounded by the right-hand side of (
10.91
) using (
10.43
). Similarly, using (
2.32
) and (
2.33
) and (
10.84
) we deduce that

(10.96)

\begin{split}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\big(\|\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}+\|\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}_{R},\,\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a},\vec{v}))\boldsymbol{J}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\big)\end{split}

is bounded by the right-hand side of (
10.89
). Lastly,

(10.97)

\begin{split}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\big(\|\langle\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}+\|\langle\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}_{R},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\big)\end{split}

is bounded by the right-hand side of (
10.89
) by (
10.83
). The bounds (
10.89
) follow from the preceding estimates and the uniform boundedness of the operator norm of the matrix
(\mathcal{M}(\vec{a},\vec{v})^{-1})^{\intercal}
for
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
with
\rho(\vec{a},\vec{v})\leq\eta_{0}
.

The bound (
10.66
) for
\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{I}-\boldsymbol{h}_{I}
follow from (
10.73
) and (
10.84
). The bounds (
10.67
) and (
10.68
) for the parameters follow from (
10.75
), (
10.77
), and (
10.89
).
∎

Lemma 10.8
.

Let
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
and
(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})
satisfy (
10.2
).
Let
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})
be the solution of (
10.21
)
obtained in Lemma
10.5
associated to
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
, and let
(\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp},\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp},\vec{\mu}^{\sharp})
be the solution associated to
(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})
.
For all
\gamma<2
there exists
C_{\gamma}>0
such that

(10.98)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}-\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}&\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}),\end{split}

and the parameters satisfy

(10.99)

\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\|(\lambda^{\sharp}_{k}-\lambda_{k})+(-1)^{k}(v^{\sharp}_{k}-v_{k})\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\\
\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}),

and,

(10.100)

\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\|(\mu^{\sharp}_{k}-\mu_{k})+(-1)^{k}(M^{-1}F_{k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp},\vec{v}^{\sharp})-M^{-1}F_{k}({\vec{a}},{\vec{v}}))\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\\
\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}}).

Proof.

We decompose
{\boldsymbol{h}}={\boldsymbol{h}}_{I}+{\boldsymbol{h}}_{*}
and
\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}=\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{I}+\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*}
, as explained above.
Let
\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*}-\boldsymbol{h}_{*}
,
\lambda^{\flat}\coloneqq\vec{\lambda}^{\sharp}_{*}-\vec{\lambda}_{*}
and

\mu^{\flat}\coloneqq\vec{\mu}^{\sharp}_{*}-\vec{\mu}_{*}
.
Taking the difference of (
10.64
) and the analogous equation for
\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*}
,
we obtain that
\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}
satisfies

(10.101)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}(t)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}(t)+\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}(t)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda^{\flat}_{k}(t)\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+\mu^{\flat}_{k}(t)\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t),

with

(10.102)

\begin{split}\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle,\quad\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k},\rangle\end{split}

and
where
\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}=\boldsymbol{f}_{l}+\boldsymbol{f}_{q}+\boldsymbol{f}_{m}
,

(10.103)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}_{l}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\boldsymbol{J}(\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t),\vec{v}^{\sharp}(t)))-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))))\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*}(t),

(10.104)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}_{q}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq J\big(\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t),\vec{v}^{\sharp}(t))+\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{I}(t)+\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*}(t))-\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t),\vec{v}^{\sharp}(t)))

\displaystyle-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t),\vec{v}^{\sharp}(t)))(\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{I}(t)+\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*}(t))\big)

\displaystyle-J\big(\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\boldsymbol{h}_{I}(t)+\boldsymbol{h}_{*}(t))-\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))

\displaystyle-\mathrm{D}^{2}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t)))(\boldsymbol{h}_{I}(t)+\boldsymbol{h}_{*}(t))\big),

and

(10.105)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}_{m}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda^{\sharp}_{*,k}(\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}(t)-\partial_{a_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t))+\mu^{\sharp}_{*,k}(\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}^{\sharp}_{k}(t)-\partial_{v_{k}}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)).

Using (
10.63
), we obtain

(10.106)

\|\boldsymbol{f}_{l}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}(\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}),

for all
\gamma<2
.
This is the worst term: for
\boldsymbol{f}_{q}
and
\boldsymbol{f}_{m}
an analogous estimate holds for
\gamma<3
. Setting

(10.107)
