\begin{split}\delta^{\flat}=\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*},\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle)^{2}+(\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{h}^{\sharp}_{*},\,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\sharp}_{k}\rangle)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

it follows from Lemma
10.1
, the above, and (
10.63
) that

(10.108)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{\flat}\|_{N_{\gamma}(\mathcal{E})}\lesssim_{\gamma}\|\boldsymbol{f}^{\flat}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}(\mathcal{E})}+\|\delta^{\flat}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\lesssim_{\gamma}\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{L^{\infty}},\end{split}

for all
\gamma<2
. Arguing exactly as in the proof of (
10.89
) it follows as well that

(10.109)

\begin{split}\|\vec{\lambda}^{\flat}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}+\|\vec{\mu}^{\flat}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\lesssim_{\gamma}\|\vec{a}^{\sharp}-\vec{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{v}^{\sharp}-\vec{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\vec{a}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{a}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\vec{v}^{\sharp})^{\prime}-\vec{v}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}.\end{split}

Using the conclusions of Lemma
10.7
and the previous two estimates completes the proof.
∎

11.
Analysis of the bifurcation equation

Recall that our goal is to study solutions to (
1.10
) of the form

(11.1)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\phi}(t,x)=\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t);x)+\boldsymbol{h}(t,x)\end{split}

with trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
as in (
10.2
) and such that the error satisfies
\lim_{t\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{h}(t,x)\|_{\mathcal{E}}=0
and the trajectories satisfy
\lim_{t\to\infty}\rho(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))=0
. The relationship between the trajectories and the error is fixed by requiring the orthogonality conditions (
9.21
).
We can thus rewrite (
1.10
) as the following coupled equations for
(\boldsymbol{h}(t),\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
:

(11.2)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{h}(t)&=\boldsymbol{J}\mathrm{D}E(\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))+\boldsymbol{h}(t))-\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k}\big(a_{k}^{\prime}(t)\partial_{a}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t)+v_{k}^{\prime}(t)\partial_{v}\boldsymbol{H}_{k}(t))\\
0&=\langle\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}(t),\boldsymbol{h}(t)\rangle,\quad\forall\,k.\end{split}

In view of Lemma
10.5
, we see that solving (
11.2
) is equivalent to finding trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
so that the triplet
(\boldsymbol{h}(t),\vec{\lambda}(t),\vec{\mu}(t))
given by Lemma
10.5
satisfies

(11.3)

\begin{split}a_{k}^{\prime}(t)=-(-1)^{k}\lambda_{k}(t),\quad v_{k}^{\prime}(t)=-(-1)^{k}\mu_{k}(t)\end{split}

for each
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. This is achieved in the following proposition.

Proposition 11.1

(Solution of the bifurcation equations)
.

There exist
\tau_{0},\eta_{0}>0
such that for all
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
and
a_{1,0},\ldots a_{n,0}
real numbers satisfying

(11.4)

\max_{1\leq k<n}\bigg|\big(a_{k+1,0}-a_{k,0}\big)-\Big(2\log(\kappa t_{0})-\log\frac{Mk(n-k)}{2}\Big)\bigg|<\eta_{0}

there exists a unique pair of trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
satisfying (
10.2
) with
\vec{a}(t_{0})=\vec{a}_{0}
and

(11.5)

a_{k}^{\prime}(t)+(-1)^{k}\lambda_{k}(t)=v_{k}^{\prime}(t)+(-1)^{k}\mu_{k}(t)=0,\qquad\text{for all }k,

where
(\boldsymbol{h}(t),\vec{\lambda}(t),\vec{\mu}(t))
is the solution associated to
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
given by Lemma
10.5
.

The remainder of this section is devoted to proving Propostion
11.1
.

11.1.
Linearized problem

Consider the equation

(11.6)

\begin{split}a_{k}^{\prime}(t)&=v_{k}(t)\\
Mv_{k}^{\prime}(t)&=2\kappa^{2}\big(e^{-(a_{k+1}(t)-a_{k}(t))}-e^{-(a_{k}(t)-a_{k-1}(t))}\big).\end{split}

This has an explicit solution (defined up to translation) by the formulas

(11.7)

\begin{split}a_{k+1}(t)-a_{k}(t)=2\log(\kappa t)-\log\Big(\frac{Mk(n-k)}{2}\Big),\quad v_{k}(t)=-\frac{n+1-2k}{t}.\end{split}

In what follows we fix the solution
(\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t),\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t))
of (
11.6
) satisfying (
11.7
) with barycenter at the origin (i.e., we require that
\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{\mathrm{expl},k}(t)=0
for each
t
, but this is an arbitrary choice).

The proof of Proposition
11.1
begins with an analysis of the linearization of the bifurcation equations about the solution
(\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t),\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t))
. Define

(11.8)

\begin{split}F_{\mathrm{expl},k}(\vec{a})=2\kappa^{2}\big(e^{-(a_{k+1}(t)-a_{k}(t))}-e^{-(a_{k}(t)-a_{k-1}(t))}\big).\end{split}

The Jacobian matrix of
\vec{F}_{\mathrm{expl}}(\vec{a})
evaluated at the explicit solution
\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t)
is given by

(11.9)

\begin{split}\mathrm{D}\vec{F}_{\mathrm{expl}}(\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t))=Mt^{-2}\mathscr{U},\end{split}

where the matrix
\mathscr{U}=\mathscr{U}_{n}=(u_{jk})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is defined by

(11.10)

u_{k,k+1}\coloneqq-k(n-k),\quad u_{k+1,k}\coloneqq-k(n-k),\quad u_{j,k}=0\ \text{ if }\ |j-k|\geq 2,

and the elements on the diagonal are determined by the requirement that the sum of the entries in each row equals 0, that is

(11.11)

u_{k,k}\coloneqq-u_{k,k-1}-u_{k,k+1}=(k-1)(n-k+1)+k(n-k),

where here and later we adopt the convention

(11.12)

u_{0,1}=u_{1,0}=u_{n+1,n}=u_{n,n+1}=0.

Note that (
11.10
) still holds for
k=0
and
k=n
.

Definition 11.2
.

We define the
Legendre vectors

\vec{P}_{0},\vec{P}_{1},\ldots,\vec{P}_{n-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
as follows:

•

\vec{P}_{0}\coloneqq(1,1,\ldots,1)
,

•

for
1\leq\ell\leq n-1
,
\vec{P}_{\ell}
is the orthogonal projection of
(0^{\ell},1^{\ell},\ldots,(n-1)^{\ell})

on the space orthogonal to
\vec{P}_{0},\ldots,\vec{P}_{\ell-1}
.

Proposition 11.3
.

For
\ell\in\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
it holds

(11.13)

\mathscr{U}\vec{P}_{\ell}=\ell(\ell+1)\vec{P}_{\ell}.

Proof.

An explicit expansion of all the terms as polynomials with respect to
j-1
reveals that

(11.14)

\sum_{k=1}^{n}u_{j,k}(k-1)^{\ell}=\ell(\ell+1)(j-1)^{\ell}+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-1}c_{n,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(j-1)^{\ell^{\prime}},

where
c_{n,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
are some coefficients whose precise form is not needed here
(it is only crucial that they do not depend on
j
).
The conclusion thus follows from the theory of symmetric matrices.
∎

In the next lemma we study the bifurcation equations linearized around the explicit trajectory
(if we translate in space the explicit trajectory, the linearized equations are unchanged).

Lemma 11.4
.

Let
t_{0}\geq 1
and let
\vec{F}=(F_{1},\dots,F_{n})
and
\vec{G}=(G_{1},\dots,G_{n})
be such that
F_{j},G_{j}\in L^{1}([t_{0},\infty);\mathbb{R})
for each
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. Let
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in(1,2)
and assume that
F_{j}\in N_{\gamma_{1}+1}
for each
j\in{1,\dots,n}
and assume
G_{j}\in N_{2}
for each
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. Let
\vec{B}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Define the projection coefficients of
\vec{F}
,
\vec{G}
onto the Legendre vectors by

(11.15)

\begin{split}f_{\ell}(t)\coloneqq|\vec{P}_{\ell}|^{-2}\langle\vec{F}(t),\,\vec{P}_{\ell}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}},\quad g_{\ell}(t)\coloneqq|\vec{P}_{\ell}|^{-2}\langle\vec{G}(t),\,\vec{P}_{\ell}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\end{split}

for
\ell=\{0,\dots,n-1\}
.
Assume further that

(11.16)

\begin{split}g_{0}(t)=\frac{1}{n}(G_{1}(t)+\dots+G_{n}(t))\end{split}

satisfies
g_{0}\in N_{\gamma_{2}+1}
.
The linear system

(11.17)

\vec{B}^{\prime}(t)=\vec{W}(t)+\vec{F}(t),\qquad\vec{W}^{\prime}(t)=(t_{0}+t)^{-2}\mathscr{U}\vec{B}(t)+\vec{G}(t)

has a unique solution that remains bounded as
t\to\infty
and such that
\vec{B}(0)=\vec{B}_{0}
. Moreover, there exists
C_{0}=C_{0}(n)\geq 0
such that it satisfies the estimates

(11.18)

\begin{split}|\vec{B}(t)|\leq C\Big(|\vec{B}_{0}|+t_{0}^{-\gamma_{1}}\|\vec{F}\|_{N_{\gamma_{1}+1}}+\|\vec{G}\|_{N_{2}}+t_{0}^{-\gamma_{2}+1}\|g_{0}\|_{N_{\gamma_{2}+1}}\Big)\qquad\text{for all }t\geq 0,\end{split}

for each
k=1,\dots,n-1
, as well as

(11.19)

\begin{split}\|\vec{W}(t)\|_{N_{1}}\leq C_{0}\Big(|\vec{B}_{0}|+t_{0}^{-\gamma_{1}}\|\vec{F}\|_{N_{\gamma_{1}+1}}+\|\vec{G}\|_{N_{2}}+t_{0}^{-\gamma_{2}+1}\|g_{0}\|_{N_{\gamma_{2}+1}}\Big)\end{split}

Proof.

Decomposing everything in the eigenbasis of
\mathscr{U}
, we obtain the system

(11.20)

b_{\ell}^{\prime}(t)=w_{\ell}(t)+f_{\ell}(t),\qquad w_{\ell}^{\prime}(t)=\ell(\ell+1)(t_{0}+t)^{-2}b_{\ell}(t)+g_{\ell}(t).

This is a standard Euler differential equation. A particular solution is given by

(11.21)

\displaystyle b_{\ell}^{\mathrm{p}}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{(t_{0}+t)^{-\ell}}{2\ell+1}\int_{0}^{t}\big((\ell+1)(t_{0}+s)^{\ell}f_{\ell}(s)-(t_{0}+s)^{\ell+1}g_{\ell}(s)\big)\mathrm{\,d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{(t_{0}+t)^{\ell+1}}{2\ell+1}\int_{t}^{\infty}\big(\ell(t_{0}+s)^{-\ell-1}f_{\ell}(s)-(t_{0}+s)^{-\ell}g_{\ell}(s)\big)\mathrm{\,d}s

and

(11.22)

\displaystyle w_{\ell}^{\mathrm{p}}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{-\ell(t_{0}+t)^{-\ell-1}}{2\ell+1}\int_{0}^{t}\big((\ell+1)(t_{0}+s)^{\ell}f_{\ell}(s)-(t_{0}+s)^{\ell+1}g_{\ell}(s)\big)\mathrm{\,d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{(\ell+1)(t_{0}+t)^{\ell}}{2\ell+1}\int_{t}^{\infty}\big(\ell(t_{0}+s)^{-\ell-1}f_{\ell}(s)-(t_{0}+s)^{-\ell}g_{\ell}(s)\big)\mathrm{\,d}s.

The solutions of the homogenous equation which are bounded are spanned by
(t_{0}+t)^{-\ell}
,
and we add an appropriate multiple so that the initial condition is satisfied. Indeed, for each initial data
\vec{B}(0)=\vec{B}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
the unique bounded solution
(\vec{B}(t),W(t))=(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}b_{\ell}(t)\vec{P}_{\ell},\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}w_{\ell}(t)\vec{P}_{\ell})
with
\vec{B}(0)=\vec{B}_{0}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{N-1}b_{0,\ell}\vec{P}_{\ell}
is given by

(11.23)

\begin{split}b_{\ell}(t)&=t_{0}^{\ell}(b_{0,\ell}-b^{\mathrm{p}}_{\ell}(0))(t_{0}+t)^{-\ell}+b_{\ell}^{\mathrm{p}}(t)\\
w_{\ell}(t)&=-\ell t_{0}^{\ell}(b_{0,\ell}-b^{\mathrm{p}}_{\ell}(0))(t_{0}+t)^{-\ell-1}+w_{\ell}^{\mathrm{p}}(t)\end{split}

For
\ell=0
we have

(11.24)

\begin{split}b_{0}(t)-b_{0,0}&=b_{0}^{\mathrm{p}}(t)-b_{0}^{\mathrm{p}}(0)\\
&=\int_{0}^{t}\big(f_{0}(s)-(t_{0}+s)g_{0}(s)\big)\mathrm{\,d}s-(t_{0}+t)\int_{t}^{\infty}g_{0}(s)\mathrm{\,d}s+t_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}g_{0}(s)\mathrm{\,d}s,\end{split}

and hence

(11.25)

\begin{split}|b_{0}(t)-b_{0,0}|\lesssim t_{0}^{-\gamma_{1}}\|\vec{F}\|_{N_{\gamma_{1}+1}}+t_{0}^{-\gamma_{2}+1}\|g_{0}\|_{N_{\gamma_{2}+1}}.\end{split}

For
\ell\geq 1
we have

(11.26)

\begin{split}b_{\ell}(t)-\frac{t_{0}^{\ell}}{(t_{0}+t)^{\ell}}b_{0,\ell}&=-\frac{t_{0}^{\ell}}{(t_{0}+t)^{\ell}}b^{\mathrm{p}}_{\ell}(0)+b_{\ell}^{\mathrm{p}}(t),\end{split}

and thus

(11.27)

\begin{split}|b_{\ell}(t)-\frac{t_{0}^{\ell}}{(t_{0}+t)^{\ell}}b_{0,\ell}|\lesssim t_{0}^{-\gamma_{1}}\|\vec{F}\|_{N_{\gamma_{1}+1}}+\|\vec{G}\|_{N_{2}}\end{split}

which gives (
11.18
). Next, for
\ell=0
, we have

(11.28)

\begin{split}(t_{0}+t)|w_{0}(t)|\leq(t_{0}+t)|w_{0}^{p}(t)|\leq(t_{0}+t)\int_{t}^{\infty}|g_{0}(s)|\,\mathrm{\,d}s\lesssim(t_{0}+t)^{-\gamma_{2}+1}\|g_{0}\|_{N_{\gamma_{2}+1}}\end{split}

If
\ell\geq 1
,

(11.29)

\begin{split}(t_{0}+t)|w_{\ell}(t)|&\leq\frac{\ell t_{0}^{\ell}}{(t_{0}+t)^{\ell}}|b_{0,\ell}|+\frac{\ell t_{0}^{\ell}}{(t_{0}+t)^{\ell}}|b_{\ell}^{p}(0)|+(t_{0}+t)|w_{\ell}^{p}(t)|\\
&\lesssim|b_{0,\ell}|+t_{0}^{-\gamma_{1}}\|f_{\ell}\|_{N_{\gamma_{1}+1}}+\|g_{\ell}\|_{N_{2}}\end{split}

completing the proof of (
11.19
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
11.1
.

We write
\vec{a}(t)=\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t)+\vec{b}(t)
and
\vec{v}(t)=\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t)+\vec{w}(t)
, where
(\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t),\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t))
is the explicit solution
of the
n
-body problem defined by (
11.7
) with the barycenter at
0
(this last condition is an arbitrary choice and could be any number). The idea is to set up a contraction mapping amongst perturbations of this explicit trajectory that are small in the norm appearing on the right hand side in Lemma
10.8
.

Let
\eta_{0}>0
be a small number and
\tau_{0}>0
be a large number, both to be fixed below. Let
\vec{a}_{0}
be as in (
11.4
), and assume (after translating) that
\vec{a}_{0}
also has barycenter
=0
like the explicit trajectory
\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t)
. Let
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
and we consider trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
, satisfying (
10.2
) with
\vec{a}(t_{0})=\vec{a}_{0}
. For
t_{0}
as above and trajectories
(\vec{b},\vec{w})\in C^{1}([t_{0},\infty);\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
\vec{b}(t_{0})=\vec{b}_{0}\coloneqq\vec{a}(t_{0})-\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}(t_{0})
, define the norm
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}([t_{0},\infty)
by

(11.30)

\begin{split}\|(\vec{b},\vec{w})\|_{\mathcal{X}}\coloneqq\|\vec{b}\|_{L^{\infty}([t_{0},\infty))}+\|\vec{b}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}([t_{0},\infty))}+\|\vec{w}\|_{N_{1}([t_{0},\infty))}+\|\vec{w}^{\prime}\|_{N_{1}([t_{0},\infty))}.\end{split}

Considering only trajectories that have
\vec{b}(t_{0})=\vec{b}_{0}
, for
\eta>0
we denote the
\eta
-ball in
\mathcal{X}
by

(11.31)

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{X}}(\eta)\coloneqq\{(\vec{b},\vec{w})\in C^{1}([t_{0},\infty);\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\mid\|(\vec{b},\vec{w})\|_{\mathcal{X}}<\eta{\ \ \text{and}\ \ }\vec{b}(t_{0})=\vec{b}_{0}\}.\end{split}

To trajectories
(\vec{a},\vec{v})=(\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{b},\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{w})
with
(\vec{b},\vec{w})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{X}}(\eta)
we associate new trajectories
(\vec{\mathfrak{a}},\vec{\mathfrak{v}})=(\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{\mathfrak{b}},\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{\mathfrak{w}})

defined as the solution of the system

(11.32)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{k}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{w}_{k}(t)-\big((-1)^{k}\lambda_{k}(t)+v_{k}(t)\big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{k}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{\mathfrak{b}}(t))_{k}-\big((-1)^{k}\mu_{k}(t)+M^{-1}F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}})+(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{b}(t))_{k}\big).

where on the right-hand side,
(\vec{\lambda}(t),\vec{\mu}(t))
is determined by
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
via Lemma
10.5
.
We denote this association as a mapping
\Phi(\vec{b}(t),\vec{w}(t))=(\vec{\mathfrak{b}}(t),\vec{\mathfrak{w}}(t))
. We will show that there exist
\eta_{0}>0
sufficiently small and
\tau_{0}>0
sufficiently large the mapping
\Phi
defines a contraction on
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{X}}(8C_{0}\eta_{0})
where
C_{0}
is the constant from Lemma
11.4
.

First we show that
\Phi:\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{X}}(8C_{0}\eta_{0})\to\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{X}}(8C_{0}\eta_{0})
. Setting up an application of Lemma
11.4
we expand the forcing on right-hand side of the equation for
\mathfrak{w}_{k}
as follows, defining

(11.33)

\begin{split}-G_{k}(t)\coloneqq(-1)^{k}\mu_{k}(t)&+M^{-1}F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}})+(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{b}(t))_{k}\\
&=(-1)^{k}\mu_{k}(t)+M^{-1}F_{k}((\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{b},\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{w})\\
&\quad-M^{-1}\Big(F_{k}(\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{b},\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{w})-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}+\vec{b})\Big)\\
&\quad-M^{-1}\Big(F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}+\vec{b})-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}})-(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{b}(t))_{k}\Big)\end{split}

By (
10.22
) we have

(11.34)

\begin{split}\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\Big(\|(-1)^{k}\lambda_{k}+v_{k}\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}+\|(-1)^{k}\mu_{k}(t)+M^{-1}F_{k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}+\vec{b})\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\Big)\leq 1\end{split}

for each
\gamma<2
, provided
t_{0}
is sufficiently large. By (
2.76
), we have

(11.35)

\begin{split}\|F_{k}(\vec{a}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{b},\vec{v}_{\mathrm{expl}}+\vec{w})-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}+\vec{b})\|_{N_{3}}\leq 1\end{split}

provided
t_{0}
is large enough. Using Taylor’s formula with remainder we find a constant
C=C(n)
so that

(11.36)

\begin{split}\|F_{\textrm{expl}}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}+\vec{b})-F_{\textrm{expl}}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}})-(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{b}(t))\|_{N_{2}}\leq C\|\vec{b}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.\end{split}

And finally observe that

(11.37)

\begin{split}\langle\vec{F}_{\textrm{expl}}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}+\vec{b})-\vec{F}_{\textrm{expl}}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}})-(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{b}(t)),\,\vec{P}_{0}\rangle=0\end{split}

which, combined with (
11.34
) and (
11.35
) gives

(11.38)

\begin{split}\|\langle\vec{G},\,\vec{P}_{0}\rangle\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq 1\end{split}

for each
\gamma<2
, provided
t_{0}
is sufficiently large.

An application of Lemma
11.4
together with the above estimates yields

(11.39)

\begin{split}\|(\vec{\mathfrak{b}},\vec{\mathfrak{w}})\|_{\mathcal{X}}<8C_{0}\eta_{0}\end{split}

after taking
t_{0}
sufficiently large and
\eta_{0}>0
sufficiently small, and making use of the equation for the estimates for
\vec{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime},\vec{\mathfrak{w}}
.

We address the contraction property. Consider two trajectories
(\vec{b},\vec{w}),(\vec{b}^{\sharp}\vec{w}^{\sharp})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{X}}(8C_{0}\eta_{0})
and their images
(\vec{\mathfrak{b}},\vec{\mathfrak{w}}),(\vec{\mathfrak{b}}^{\sharp},\vec{\mathfrak{w}}^{\sharp})
under the mapping
\Phi
. Then
(\vec{\mathfrak{b}}^{\flat},\vec{\mathfrak{w}}^{\flat})\coloneqq(\vec{\mathfrak{b}}^{\sharp},\vec{\mathfrak{w}}^{\sharp})-(\vec{\mathfrak{b}},\vec{\mathfrak{w}})
satisfies the equation,

(11.40)

\begin{split}(\mathfrak{b}^{\flat}_{k})^{\prime}(t)&=\mathfrak{w}^{\flat}_{k}(t)-\Big(\big((-1)^{k}\lambda^{\sharp}_{k}(t)+v^{\sharp}_{k}(t)\big)-\big((-1)^{k}\lambda_{k}(t)+v_{k}(t)\big),\\
(\mathfrak{w}^{\flat}_{k})^{\prime}(t)&=(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{\mathfrak{b}}^{\flat}(t))_{k}\\
&\quad-\Big(\big((-1)^{k}\mu^{\sharp}_{k}(t)+M^{-1}F_{k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t),\vec{v}^{\sharp}(t))\big)-\big((-1)^{k}\mu_{k}(t)+M^{-1}F_{k}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\big)\Big)\\
&\quad-M^{-1}\Big(\big(F_{k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t),\vec{v}^{\sharp}(t))-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t))\big)-\big(F_{k}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{(}t))-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}(t))\big)\Big)\\
&\quad-M^{-1}\Big(F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t))-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}(t))-(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{b}(t)^{\sharp})_{k}\Big)\\
&\quad+M^{-1}\Big(F_{\textrm{expl},k}({\vec{a}}(t))-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}(t))-(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}{\vec{b}(t)})_{k}\Big)\end{split}

with zero initial data. The estimates (
10.99
) and (
10.100
) give that

(11.41)

\begin{split}\|\big((-1)^{k}\lambda^{\sharp}_{k}(t)+v^{\sharp}_{k}(t)\big)-\big((-1)^{k}\lambda_{k}(t)+v_{k}(t)\big)\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}\|(\vec{b}^{\sharp},\vec{w}^{\sharp})-(\vec{b},\vec{w})\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
\|\big((-1)^{k}(\mu^{\sharp}_{k}(t)-\mu_{k}(t)\big)+M^{-1}\big(F_{k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t),\vec{v}^{\sharp}(t))\big)-F_{k}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))\big)\|_{N_{\gamma+1}}\leq C_{\gamma}\|(\vec{b}^{\sharp},\vec{w}^{\sharp})-(\vec{b},\vec{w})\|_{\mathcal{X}}\end{split}

for each
\gamma\in[1,2)
. We also note the estimates,

(11.42)

\begin{split}\|\big(F_{k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t),\vec{v}^{\sharp}(t))-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t))\big)-\big(F_{k}(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}(t))\big)\|_{N_{3}}\lesssim\|\vec{b}^{\sharp}-\vec{b}\|_{L^{\infty}}\end{split}

and

(11.43)

\Big\|\big(F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}(t))-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}(t))-(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}\vec{b}(t)^{\sharp})_{k}\big)\\
-\big(F_{\textrm{expl},k}({\vec{a}}(t))-F_{\textrm{expl},k}(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}(t))-(t_{0}+t)^{-2}(\mathscr{U}{\vec{b}(t)})_{k}\Big)\Big\|_{N_{2}}\lesssim(\|\vec{b}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\vec{b}\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\vec{b}^{\sharp}-\vec{b}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

As before, we observe that the orthogonal projections of the last two lines of (
11.40
) onto
\vec{P}_{0}
vanishes identically. Applying Lemma
11.4
to (
11.40
), noting
\vec{b}^{\sharp}(t_{0})-\vec{b}(t_{0})=\vec{0}
and using the estimates above we find that

(11.44)

\begin{split}\|(\vec{\mathfrak{b}}^{\sharp},\vec{\mathfrak{w}}^{\sharp})-(\vec{\mathfrak{b}},\vec{\mathfrak{w}})\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|(\vec{b}^{\sharp},\vec{w}^{\sharp})-(\vec{b},\vec{w})\|_{\mathcal{X}}\end{split}

provided
\eta_{0}
is taken sufficiently small and
t_{0}
is sufficiently large (and again making use of the equation for the estimates for the derivatves). The contraction mapping principle yields a unique trajectory
(\vec{b},\vec{w})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{X}}(8C_{0}\eta_{0})
such that
\Phi(\vec{b},\vec{w})=(\vec{b},\vec{w})
. By Lemma
11.4
this is the unique solution to (
11.40
) amongst those trajectories
(\vec{a},\vec{v})
satisfying (
10.2
) with initial data
\vec{a}(t_{0})=\vec{a}_{0}
proving Proposition
11.1
.
∎

We conclude this section with a quick corollary of Proposition
11.1
and its proof.
Recall the definition of the open set
\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0})
from (
1.35
).
Proposition
11.1
then defines a mapping

(11.45)

\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0})\ni\vec{a}_{0}\\
\mapsto(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0}),\vec{a}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0}),\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0}))\in N_{1}(\mathcal{E})\times C^{1}([t_{0},\infty);\mathbb{R}^{n})\times C^{1}([t_{0},\infty);\mathbb{R}^{n})

where
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
is any choice of initial time for which (
11.4
) holds, and
(\vec{a}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0}),\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0}))
are the trajectories provided by Proposition
11.1
and
(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0}),\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0}),\vec{\mu}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0}))
is the solution to (
10.21
) provided by Lemma
10.5
with
\lambda_{k}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0})=-(-1)^{k}a_{k}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0})
and
\mu_{k}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0})=-(-1)^{k}v_{k}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0})
. It is evident from the proof that given
\vec{a}_{0}\in\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0})
and
\eta_{0}
as in Proposition
11.1
, two choices of times
t_{0},\widetilde{t}_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
for which (
11.4
) holds lead to trajectories that are time translations of each other, that is if
\widetilde{t}_{0}\geq t_{0}
,

(11.46)

(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{a}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t))\\
=(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0},\widetilde{t}_{0};\widetilde{t}_{0}+t-t_{0}),\vec{a}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};\widetilde{t}_{0}+t-t_{0}),\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};\widetilde{t}_{0}+t-t_{0}))

Corollary 11.5
.

The mapping defined by (
11.45
) is continuous.

Proof.

Consider two initial data
\vec{a}_{0},\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}\in\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0})
. By taking
|\vec{a}-\vec{a}^{\sharp}|
sufficiently small we can ensure that (
11.4
) is satisfied with the same choice of
t_{0}
for both
\vec{a}
and
\vec{a}^{\sharp}
. Consider their images
(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0}),\vec{a}(\vec{a}_{0}),\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0}))
and
(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}),\vec{a}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}),\vec{v}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}))
under the mapping defined by (
11.45
), noting we have removed
t_{0}
from the notation to simplify it. We can assume (after a spatial translation) that
\sum_{k}a_{0,k}=0
and
\sum_{k}a^{\sharp}_{0,k}=0
so that we can use the explicit trajectory
(\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}},\vec{v}_{\textrm{expl}})
as a reference for both, writing

(11.47)

\begin{split}\vec{a}(\vec{a}_{0};t)&=\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}(t)+\vec{b}(\vec{a}_{0};t),\quad\vec{a}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0};t)=\vec{a}_{\textrm{expl}}(t)+\vec{b}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0};t)\\
\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0};t)&=\vec{v}_{\textrm{expl}}(t)+\vec{w}(\vec{a}_{0};t),\quad\vec{v}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0};t)=\vec{v}_{\textrm{expl}}(t)+\vec{w}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0};t)\end{split}

Using the same argument used to establish the estimate (
11.44
) we obtain the estimate

(11.48)

\|(\vec{b}(\vec{a}_{0}),\vec{w}(\vec{a}_{0}))-(\vec{b}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}),\vec{w}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}))\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
\leq C|\vec{b}_{0}(\vec{a}_{0};t_{0})-\vec{b}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0};t_{0})|+\frac{1}{2}\|(\vec{b}(\vec{a}_{0}),\vec{w}(\vec{a}_{0}))-(\vec{b}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}),\vec{w}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}))\|_{\mathcal{X}}

After absorbing the second term on the right above into the left, we may use the above together with (
10.98
) to obtain

(11.49)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0})-\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0})\|_{N_{1}}\leq C\|(\vec{b}(\vec{a}_{0}),\vec{w}(\vec{a}_{0}))-(\vec{b}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}),\vec{w}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0}))\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq C|\vec{b}_{0}(\vec{a}_{0};t_{0})-\vec{b}(\vec{a}^{\sharp}_{0};t_{0})|.\end{split}

This completes the proof.
∎

12.
Proof of the uniqueness of kink clusters and corollaries

We conclude with the proofs of Theorem
3
, Corollary
1
and Corollary
2
. We begin with a quick lemma, adding to the conclusions of Theorem
1
.

Lemma 12.1

(Dynamical Modulation Lemma)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)
be a kink
n
-cluster. Then there exists
t_{0},C_{0}>0
such that the following are true. The
C^{1}
modulation parameters
(\vec{a},\vec{v})=(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}))
given by Lemma
4.1
are defined on
[t_{0},\infty):\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
and satisfy

(12.1)

\begin{split}|\vec{a}\,^{\prime}(t)-\vec{v}(t)|+|\vec{v}\,^{\prime}(t)|\leq C_{0}t^{-2}\end{split}

for all
t\geq t_{0}
.

Proof.

Choose
t_{0}
sufficiently large so that
{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))<\eta_{0}
for all
t\in[t_{0},\infty)
, where
\eta_{0}>0
is as in Lemma
1.5
. Define

(12.2)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{h}(t)=\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)).\end{split}

By Theorem
1
we have

(12.3)

\begin{split}\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{1}(\mathcal{E})}<\delta\end{split}

provided
t_{0}
is sufficiently large, and where
\delta>0
is as in Lemma
10.5
. Because
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)
solves (
1.10
) we see that the triplet
(\boldsymbol{h}(t),\vec{\lambda}(t),\vec{\mu}(t))
with

(12.4)

\begin{split}\lambda_{k}(t)=-(-1)^{k}a_{k}(t){\ \ \text{and}\ \ }\mu_{k}(t)=-(-1)^{k}v_{k}(t)\end{split}

solves (
10.21
). However, while the trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
satisfy (
1.31
) we do not yet know that they satisfy (
10.2
), particularly we have not yet established (
12.1
). To see this, we use the exact same argument used to establish the estimates (
10.22
) with
\gamma=1
(i.e., via (
10.8
)). Using (
12.4
) we obtain

(12.5)

\begin{split}\|a_{k}^{\prime}-v_{k}\|_{N_{2}}\lesssim 1{\ \ \text{and}\ \ }\|v_{k}^{\prime}-F_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\|_{N_{2}}\lesssim 1\end{split}

for each
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.
The first bound is the same as the required estimate for the first term in (
12.1
). Using (
1.31
) we see that

(12.6)

\begin{split}\max_{k}\|F_{k}(\vec{a},\vec{v})\|_{N_{2}}\lesssim 1\end{split}

and combining this with the above we obtain
\max_{k}\|v_{k}^{\prime}\|_{N_{2}}\lesssim 1
, which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of the Theorem
3
.

Let
\tau_{0},\eta_{0}>0
to be fixed below,
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
and let
\vec{a}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be as in (
1.32
). By taking
\eta_{0},\tau_{0}
as in Proposition
11.1
we obtain a unique pair of
C^{1}
trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))
on
[t_{0},\infty)
satisfying the conclusions of Proposition
11.1
with
a(t_{0})=\vec{a}_{0}
. In particular, using (
11.5
) and Lemma
10.5
we can thus associate to
\vec{a}_{0}
a unique solution
(\boldsymbol{h},\vec{\lambda},\vec{\mu})\in C([t_{0},\infty),\mathcal{E})\times C^{1}([t_{0},\infty),\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
to (
11.2
) with
\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{1}(\mathcal{E})}\leq\delta
and with
\lambda_{k}(t)=-(-1)^{k}a_{k}(t)
and
\mu_{k}(t)=-(-1)^{k}v_{k}(t)
. Writing the triplet
(\boldsymbol{h}(t),\vec{\lambda}(t),\vec{\mu}(t))=(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{\mu}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t))
, the trajectories
(\vec{a}(t),\vec{v}(t))=(\vec{a}(a_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t))
and setting

(12.7)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t)=\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t))+\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\end{split}

we see that
\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t)
solves (
1.10
) by construction and is a kink
n
-cluster because
\boldsymbol{h}\in N_{1}(\mathcal{E})
and because the trajectories satisfy (
10.2
).

Next we prove uniqueness. Fix
\tau_{0},\eta_{0}>0
to be determined below. Let
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
and fix any
\vec{a}_{0}
satisfying (
1.32
). Suppose that
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)\in\mathcal{E}_{(-1)^{n},1}
is a kink
n
-cluster with

{\bf d}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))<\eta_{0}
for all
t\geq t_{0}
. By taking
\eta_{0}
sufficiently small, by Lemma
1.5
we can associate to
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)
, for each
t\geq t_{0}
, unique modulation parameters
(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)))
such that (
4.6
) holds. To prove uniqueness, we suppose in addition that
\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))
satisfies
\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t_{0}))=\vec{a}_{0}
.

Defining

(12.8)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{h}(t)\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)-\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)),\end{split}

we have
\|\boldsymbol{h}\|_{N_{1}(\mathcal{E})}<\delta
(for
\delta
as in Lemma
10.5
) by Theorem
1
and
(\boldsymbol{h}(t),\vec{\lambda}(t),\vec{\mu}(t))
solve (
10.21
) with
\lambda_{k}(t)\coloneqq-(-1)^{k}a_{k}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))
and
\mu_{k}(t)\coloneqq-(-1)^{k}v_{k}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t))
for all
k
. Observe that (
10.2
) is satisfied for the trajectories
(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)))
because of (
1.31
) and (
12.1
).
Lemma
10.5
and Proposition
11.1
imply that
(\boldsymbol{h}(t),\vec{\lambda}(t),\vec{\mu}(t))=(\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{\lambda}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{\mu}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t))
and thus
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)=\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t)
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t)
be a kink
n
-cluster as in the statement of Corollary
1
and as above we write

(12.9)

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t)=\boldsymbol{H}(\vec{a}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t),\vec{v}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t))+\boldsymbol{h}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t).\end{split}

with the modulation parameters chosen as in the statement of Theorem
3
, i.e., so that (
4.6
) holds.
The estimate (
1.33
) follows from Lemma
10.5
. The estimate (
1.34
) follows from (
12.1
).
∎

We conclude with the proof of Corollary
2
.

Proof of Corollary
2
.

We start by showing that the mapping (
1.36
) is continuous. Fix
\eta_{0}
and
\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0})
as in the statement, and consider
\vec{a}_{0}\in\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0})
with (
1.32
) satisfied for some
t_{0}\geq\tau_{0}
. As usual let
\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0},t_{0};t_{0})
denote the image of
\vec{a}_{0}
under the map (
1.36
). As discussed in Remark
1.8
the image of
\vec{a}_{0}
under this map is independent of
t_{0}
and we simply write
\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0})
.

Let
\vec{a}_{0,n}\in\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0})
be a sequence with
|\vec{a}_{0}-\vec{a}_{0,n}|\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. For
|\vec{a}_{0}-\vec{a}_{0,n}|
sufficiently small, we see that (
1.32
) is satisfied for
\vec{a}_{0,n}
with the same
t_{0}
for all sufficiently large
n
. Letting
\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0,n})
denote the images under the map (
1.36
) we see that
\|\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0})-\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0,n})\|_{\mathcal{E}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
by Corollary
11.5
.

Next, consider any element of
\mathcal{M}_{n}
in the image of the mapping (
1.36
). By definition it can be written as
\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0})=\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0};t_{0},t_{0})
for some
\vec{a}_{0}\in\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0})
. By Theorem
3
, the uniquely defined modulation parameters
(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0})),\vec{v}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0})))
from Lemma
1.5
satisfy
\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}_{0}))=\vec{a}_{0}
, giving the inverse map, which is continuous by Lemma
1.5
.

Next, let
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
be any choice of initial data and let
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)
denote its forward trajectory. By (
1.31
) we see that
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)\in\mathcal{M}_{n,\operatorname{loc}}=\boldsymbol{\Phi}(\mathcal{T}(\eta_{0}))
for all sufficiently large
t
. By choosing
t_{0}
large enough, we may write
\boldsymbol{\phi}(t)=\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t_{0})),t_{0};t)
. To show that
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is a topological manifold, we still need to show that an open neighborhood of our arbitrary choice of the initial data
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
is homeomorphic to an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. But this is a consequence of the local well-posedness theory for (
1.10
) by pulling back an open neighborhood of
\boldsymbol{\phi}(\vec{a}(\boldsymbol{\phi}(t_{0})),t_{0};t_{0})
in
\mathcal{M}_{n,\operatorname{loc}}
via the continuous flow by time
t_{0}
.
∎

Jacek Jendrej

Institut de Mathématiques de Jussieu,
Sorbonne Université

4 place Jussieu,
75005 Paris, France
